This keeps `test_adoptsTheLegacyUserDefaultsId` and `test_unwritableStoreStillYieldsAnIdAndKeepsItInDefaults` (ProductionHardeningTests.swift:1869, :1893) passing — the latter uses `store.writable = false`, which now takes the branch that preserves the defaults copy. Add a test asserting `defaults.string(forKey:)` is nil after a successful write.

### **[FIXED]** freeScansRemaining is snapshotted as a final number and never resets at the UTC day boundary

`ios/SnapWorth/Services/WidgetDataStore.swift:326`

Line 326 stores `FreeScanCounter.remaining` as a literal integer. That value is only recomputed when `writeHaul` runs: app launch (`SnapWorthApp.seedWidgetData`, line 132), 600 ms after a save/delete/re-grade (`ScanRepository.scheduleWidgetSync`, line 122), or `deleteAll` (line 50). The widget's own refresh does not help — `HaulOnlyProvider.getTimeline` (MoreWidgets.swift:26-30) re-reads the same stored blob every hour, so the number cannot change without the app running.

But the underlying allowance is day-scoped and resets on its own: `FreeScanCounter.remaining` is `serverRemaining ?? max(0, Config.freeScansAllowed - used)` (ScanViewModel.swift:347), and both `serverRemaining` and `used` are gated on `isServerToday(stamped)` (ScanViewModel.swift:117-137, 149-156), which compares against the UTC calendar day. So at UTC midnight the in-app number jumps back to 1 while the widget's copy stays frozen at whatever it was written as.

The rendered consequence is in `ScansLeftView`: `headline` (MoreWidgets.swift:164), `subtitle` (MoreWidgets.swift:171-173), `circularValue` (MoreWidgets.swift:178), `accentForRemaining` (MoreWidgets.swift:182) and `spokenLabel` (MoreWidgets.swift:189-192) all read the frozen field.

**What happens.** With `Config.freeScansAllowed = 1`, a free user scans at 18:00. The blob is written with `freeScansRemaining: 0`. They do not reopen the app. At 00:00 UTC their allowance resets server-side and in-app, but the Home Screen widget renders "0" in terracotta with "Back tomorrow, or go Pro" — and keeps rendering it for the whole next day and indefinitely after, until the app is launched. The widget actively tells a user with a scan available that they have none, and points them at the paywall.

**Fix.** Stop snapshotting a derived day-scoped number. Store the inputs instead — the allowance, the used count, and the UTC day stamp the count belongs to — and have the widget re-derive `remaining` at render time against the entry's `date`, zeroing/resetting when the stored day is not the current UTC day. The payload already carries `updatedAt` (SnapWorthWidgets.swift:46) and no widget reads it, so the reader has the day stamp it needs today; at minimum, treat a blob whose `updatedAt` falls on an earlier UTC day as "allowance unknown" and render the allowance rather than 0.

### **[FIXED]** monthProfit/monthFlips are month-scoped snapshots with no invalidation, so "This month" shows last month's money

`ios/SnapWorth/Services/WidgetDataStore.swift:330`

Lines 304-313 compute the month-to-date ledger against `Calendar.current.dateInterval(of: .month, for: Date())` — the month that was current *at write time* — and lines 330-331 store the resulting `monthProfit`/`monthFlips` as plain numbers. Nothing in the widget target re-scopes them: `MonthProfitView` renders them under a hardcoded `Text("This month")` header (MoreWidgets.swift:238) via `value` (line 262-263), `caption` (line 276) and `spoken` (line 284-285), and `HaulOnlyProvider.getTimeline` (MoreWidgets.swift:26-30) only re-reads the same blob.

Because the blob is only rewritten on launch or on a scan insert/delete/re-grade, a month boundary crossed while the app is closed leaves the previous month's figures in place under a label that says they are the current month's. This is the highest-stakes instance of the problem because the number is money and the surrounding copy asserts a time period it no longer matches.

**What happens.** A Pro user sells six items across September for $214 realized profit and last opens the app on 28 September. On 3 October the Profit-this-month widget reads "$214" in sage with "from 6 flips" under the header "This month" — a month in which they have sold nothing. The in-app Flips screen (`FlipsViewModel.monthlyBuckets`, FlipsViewModel.swift:123-137) correctly shows October at $0, so the two surfaces disagree about the same money. (Today this is masked by the `isPro` defect above, which forces `monthProfit` to nil for everyone; fixing that entitlement bug makes this one user-visible.)

**Fix.** Store the month the figures belong to alongside them — e.g. add a `monthStart: Date` to `WidgetHaulData` written from `monthInterval.start` — and have `MonthProfitView` fall back to the "no flips yet" presentation when `monthStart` is not the month containing the timeline entry's `date`. `updatedAt` (SnapWorthWidgets.swift:46) already suffices as a cruder check and is currently read by nothing. Add the field to both copies of the shared block, defaulted in `init(from:)` per the rule at SnapWorthWidgets.swift:31-37.

### **[FIXED]** "Profit this month" keeps last month's figure after the month rolls over

`ios/SnapWorth/Services/WidgetDataStore.swift:414`

`writeHaul` resolves the month at *write* time (`Calendar.current.dateInterval(of: .month, for: Date())`, line 414) and stores only the resulting sum (`monthProfit`, line 440) and count (`monthFlips`, line 441). The blob records nothing about which month the figure belongs to, and nothing in the widget target ever checks: `MonthProfitView` renders it unconditionally under the fixed header "This month" (ios/SnapWorthWidgets/MoreWidgets.swift:239-263). `updatedAt` is written (line 435) and decoded (line 132) but is not read by a single view. The blob is only ever rewritten by app activity — `ScanRepository.scheduleWidgetSync` after a save/delete/re-grade, `SnapWorthApp.seedWidgetData` on launch and on an entitlement change — so nothing recomputes it while the app is closed. `HaulOnlyProvider.getTimeline` (MoreWidgets.swift:26-30) reloads hourly but only re-reads the same precomputed blob, so the reload cannot correct the number, it just redraws it.

**What happens.** A Pro user sells 6 items in September with $214 of realized profit and last opens the app on Sept 30. From Oct 1 onward, without opening the app, the "Profit this month" widget reads "This month / $214 / from 6 flips" and VoiceOver announces "$214 profit this month from 6 flips" (MoreWidgets.swift:262-263). October's true figure is $0 from 0 flips. It stays wrong for as many days as the user goes without launching the app — which is precisely the case the widget exists to serve.

**Fix.** Carry the month the figure belongs to in the blob (either a new `monthStart: Date` or reuse the already-stored `updatedAt`) and have `MonthProfitView` fall through to the empty state when `Calendar.current.isDate(haul.updatedAt, equalTo: .now, toGranularity: .month)` is false. Also cap the timeline at the next month boundary in `HaulOnlyProvider.getTimeline` (`policy: .after(min(nextHour, startOfNextMonth))`) so the widget re-renders promptly instead of up to an hour late. The model change must be made identically in both copies of the shared block (ios/SnapWorthWidgets/SnapWorthWidgets.swift), which CI diffs byte-for-byte.

### **[FIXED]** Control press is dropped entirely when SnapWorth is already foreground-active

`ios/SnapWorth/SnapWorthApp.swift:83`

The handoff has exactly two drain sites: the `.task` at SnapWorthApp.swift:78 (runs once, at scene creation) and the `scenePhase == .active` edge at line 83. `onChange` fires only on a *transition*, so if the app is already `.active` when `OpenScanIntent.perform()` runs there is no transition and no drain — the `.task` already ran at launch and never re-runs (RootView's identity is stable). `openAppWhenRun = true` (AppIntent.swift:14) resolves to a no-op when the app is already frontmost: no `onOpenURL`, no relaunch, no phase change. I confirmed with grep that lines 78 and 83 are the only two callers of `drainPendingWidgetAction()` and that there is no `UIApplication.didBecomeActiveNotification` / `willEnterForeground` observer anywhere in the app or extension target.

**What happens.** SnapWorth is frontmost on the My Flips tab. The user presses the Action Button, which iOS 18 lets them assign directly to this Control (the file header of SnapWorthWidgetsControl.swift names the Action Button as the primary use case). `perform()` writes `snapworth.widget.pendingAction = "scan"` into the App Group, the app does not relaunch and does not change scene phase, so nothing drains the key: the camera never opens and the user sees the Flips tab. The same holds for invoking the "Scan an item" intent from Shortcuts while the app is frontmost. The key then survives to be replayed on the next foreground (see the stale-replay finding).

**Fix.** Stop making delivery depend on a scene-phase edge. Simplest: drop the UserDefaults handoff for the control and use the URL route the six other widgets already use — `ControlWidgetButton(action: OpenURLIntent(URL(string: "snapworth://scan")!))` (iOS 18), or keep `OpenScanIntent` and `return .result(opensIntent: OpenURLIntent(URL(string: "snapworth://scan")!))`. `onOpenURL` (SnapWorthApp.swift:76) is delivered even to an already-frontmost app, so it covers both cold and hot cases with one path. If the App Group handoff must stay, post a Darwin notification (`CFNotificationCenterGetDarwinNotifyCenter`) from `perform()` and drain on receipt in addition to the two existing sites.

### **[FIXED]** Drained scan request is a no-op whenever ScanView already has a sheet or cover presented

`ios/SnapWorth/SnapWorthApp.swift:116`

`drainPendingWidgetAction()` posts `.snapWidgetOpenScan`, and the only observer (MainTabView.swift:69-71) does nothing but `selectedTab = 0`. ScanView's presentations are private `@State` on ScanView — `showResult`, `showThriftFlip`, `showNotifPriming`, `vm.showPaywall` (ScanView.swift:12-14, 271, 300, 303) — and nothing observes `.snapWidgetOpenScan` to clear them. `isCameraObscured` (ScanView.swift:27-28) is true while any of them is set, and the `onChange(of: isCameraObscured)` at ScanView.swift:264 keeps the capture session stopped. So when the Scan tab is already selected and obscured, the drained press changes nothing at all — yet `takePendingAction()` has already consumed it.

**What happens.** User scans an item; the result sheet is presented over the Scan tab (`showResult == true`, `selectedTab == 0`). They background the app, spot another item, and press the Control Center / Lock Screen Scan control. The app resumes, line 83 fires, the key is consumed, `.snapWidgetOpenScan` is posted, MainTabView sets `selectedTab = 0` — already 0, so no state changes. The result sheet (or the intro paywall, or the Thrift Flip full-screen cover) is still on screen and the camera session is still stopped. The press is silently swallowed. This is the main *working* delivery path, so it is not a corner case.

**Fix.** Make the scan route mean "give me a live camera", not "select tab 0". Add an observer in ScanView for `.snapWidgetOpenScan` that clears `showResult`, `showThriftFlip`, `showNotifPriming` and `vm.showPaywall`, calls `vm.reset()` and `cameraManager.capturedImage = nil`, and let MainTabView keep only the tab switch. Posting to a shared `@Observable` router with a `scanRequestID: UUID` that ScanView reacts to is the version that cannot be dropped by a view that is not yet mounted.

### **[FIXED]** CSV export dates are rendered in GMT while every other date rule in the ledger is local — evening sales export on the wrong day, month and tax year

`ios/SnapWorth/ViewModels/FlipsViewModel.swift:172`

`csv()` builds its date column with `ISO8601DateFormatter` and sets only `formatOptions = [.withFullDate]` (lines 172-173), consumed at line 177. `ISO8601DateFormatter.timeZone` defaults to GMT, so the printed day is the UTC day. Every other date rule in this feature is local: `isInCurrentMonth` uses `Calendar.current` (lines 229-232), `monthlyBuckets` uses `cal.dateInterval(of: .month, ...)` (lines 126-136), and the export's own filename via `fileStamp()` (lines 202-207) uses a `DateFormatter` with no timeZone set, i.e. the local zone. `soldDate` is stamped with wall-clock `Date()` (ResultView.swift:500) or picked from a local-calendar DatePicker (ResultView.swift:416, 486-490), so it carries a real time-of-day, not a normalized midnight.

**What happens.** A US Pacific (UTC-7) seller marks an item sold at 18:00 on 2026-08-31. soldDate = 2026-09-01T01:00Z. The app's month card and the 6-month bars count that sale in August (local calendar), but the CSV row reads Date = 2026-09-01. This fires for every sale logged after 17:00 PDT / 20:00 EDT — i.e. most evenings for most of the US user base — and at a month boundary the sale is exported into the wrong month; a 31 Dec evening sale is exported into the next tax year, in a file whose whole purpose is bookkeeping. The filename (local date) and the rows (UTC date) also disagree with each other.

**Fix.** Set `iso.timeZone = .current` after line 172, or drop ISO8601DateFormatter for a `DateFormatter` with `locale = Locale(identifier: "en_US_POSIX")`, `dateFormat = "yyyy-MM-dd"`, `calendar = Calendar.current` and the current time zone — the same construction `fileStamp()` already uses — so the exported day matches the day the app counted the sale in.

### **[FIXED]** Verdict money is rounded to whole dollars, so the fee breakdown does not add up and sub-$1 profits read "$0" under a green "Worth flipping"

`ios/SnapWorth/ViewModels/ThriftFlipViewModel.swift:187`

`money(_:)` (line 186-188) and `signedMoney(_:)` (line 190-193) format through `NumberFormatter.snapCurrency`, which is configured with `maximumFractionDigits = 0` (DesignSystem.swift:254, asserted by `test_snapCurrency_noDecimals`). That is fine for a valuation *range*, but Thrift Flip uses these two helpers for exact cent-level arithmetic that it prints as an itemised subtraction: `metric("Net profit", signedMoney(calc.netProfit))` (ThriftFlipView.swift:279), `verdictLine` (ThriftFlipView.swift:322-323) and `feeBreakdown`'s four rows (ThriftFlipView.swift:338-343). Each row is rounded independently, so the visible rows do not reconcile with the visible total, and any net profit under $1 — exactly the decision boundary where the number matters — is printed as "$0" while the headline at ThriftFlipView.swift:264 stays green because `isProfitable` is the exact `netProfit > 0`.

**What happens.** eBay selected, shop price 10.40, expected resale 20.75, shipping blank: fees = 20.75x0.1325+0.40 = 3.149375, net = 7.200625. The card shows Resale $21, − Fees $3, − Paid $10 (= $8) above a Net profit of $7. Second case: shop price 10, resale 12 -> fees 1.99, net 0.01 -> headline "Worth flipping", money line "Resell at $12 -> $0 profit after fees.", Net profit "$0", ROI "0%".

**Fix.** Do not change `snapCurrency` (its zero-decimal behaviour is deliberate and unit-tested for ranges). Add a cents formatter local to this screen — a `NumberFormatter` with `numberStyle = .currency`, `currencyCode = "USD"`, `locale = en_US`, `minimumFractionDigits = 2`, `maximumFractionDigits = 2` — and route `money`/`signedMoney` through it, so the breakdown rows and the net profit are all printed to the cent and reconcile.

### **[FIXED]** The message field on the support screen has no accessible name, and its 500-character limit is invisible to VoiceOver

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/FeedbackView.swift:98`

`TextEditor(text: $message)` takes no placeholder and carries no `.accessibilityLabel`. "Your message" at line 94 is a separate `Text` in the enclosing VStack, so it is a sibling stop, not the field's name — VoiceOver focusing the editor announces only its content, i.e. nothing at all when empty. The character counter (line 116) and the "N more characters needed" hint (line 123) are likewise unattached, so neither the 500-char cap nor the 10-char minimum is exposed as a value or hint on the control they constrain. Every other text input in the app is labelled: HistoryView.swift:99, ResultView.swift:340/441/684, ThriftFlipView.swift:415/440 — the last two added by AUDIT-2026-09-07 I-25 for exactly this reason. This is the one screen the 2026-09-11 audit rebuilt end to end as the sole support channel (E-1 to E-6).

**What happens.** With VoiceOver on, open Settings → Report a bug and swipe to the text area: it announces "text field" with no name. Type 480 characters and keep going — input silently stops at 500 (line 111-113) with no announcement, and nothing ever said there was a limit.

**Fix.** Add `.accessibilityLabel("Your message")` to the `TextEditor`, plus `.accessibilityValue("\(message.count) of \(maxChars) characters")` and `.accessibilityHint(canSend ? "" : "At least 10 characters needed to send")`. Mark the counter at line 116 `.accessibilityHidden(true)` once its content is carried by the field, and post an announcement when the cap truncates.

### **[FIXED]** "Your finds are worth" counts sold items, contradicting the "still held" line directly beneath it

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/HistoryView.swift:59`

`vm.totalValue(from: results)` (line 59) resolves to `HistoryViewModel.portfolioTotal` (HistoryViewModel.swift:50-52), which sums `priceRange(for:).likely` over **every** row with no status filter — sold items included. The insight line rendered three lines lower from `vm.insights(from: results)` (line 62) does filter: `insights` (HistoryViewModel.swift:92-103) routes `.sold` into `realized` and deliberately excludes it from `unrealized`, and `insightLine` (HistoryViewModel.swift:127) prints `"<realized> realised · <unrealized> still held"`. So the headline includes a sold item's *estimate* while the subtitle says that same item is not held. The estimate is also not the sale price, so the sold item contributes a number that corresponds to no real money anywhere. `NotificationManager.digest` (NotificationManager.swift:395) has the identical omission, so the weekly push repeats the inflated figure.

**What happens.** One item, valueLow 40 / valueHigh 60, conditionNotes "Good" (baseline `.good`, factor 1.0, likely = 50). Mark it Sold for 100, paid 20, fees 10. PortfolioBanner then renders, on one card: headline "Your finds are worth $50.00", then "1 item scanned", then "$70.00 realised · $0.00 still held". The $50 headline is the estimate of an item the same card says is no longer held, and $50 reconciles with neither $70 nor $0. With a library of sold flips the headline drifts arbitrarily far from both the realised and the held figure.

**Fix.** Exclude sold rows from the headline: in `HistoryViewModel.portfolioTotal` (HistoryViewModel.swift:51) use `Self.total(of: results.filter { $0.status != .sold }.map(\.portfolioValue))`. Apply the same filter in `NotificationManager.digest` (NotificationManager.swift:395) so the notification and the banner keep agreeing, and update `PortfolioDigestTests.test_bodyCarriesTheRealTotal` (MigrationTests.swift:673) which currently pins the unfiltered sum. If the product intent really is "lifetime worth of everything I ever scanned", then the fix is instead to make `insightLine` reconcile — but the two figures cannot both stand as they are.

### **[FIXED]** Subscribing from the Trending card shows no Pro trend content, because the /trends client cache is not keyed on entitlement

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/HistoryView.swift:240`

The `/trends` payload *shape* is decided server-side by tier: `backend/notify.py:2713-2735` adds `average_estimate` and `notable_finds` only `if is_pro`, and caches per tier under `f"{TRENDS_CACHE_KEY}:{tier}"`. The iOS cache does not carry that key — `TrendsAPIClient.cached` (ListingService.swift:349) is a single `(value, at)` tuple with a 30-minute TTL (ListingService.swift:347) and no tier component, and `fetch()` returns it before building any request (ListingService.swift:352-355). So the free-shaped payload (`notableFinds == []`, every `averageEstimate == nil`) is handed back to a user who has since become Pro. Nothing invalidates it on entitlement change: `SnapWorthApp.swift:85-94` reacts to `purchaseService.isSubscribed` to re-seed widget data — the same class of bug, already fixed there — but says nothing to `TrendsAPIClient`. HistoryView's `.task` at line 239-241 also has no `id:`, so it does not re-run when `isPro` flips, and a sheet dismissal does not re-trigger it.

**What happens.** Free user on My Finds taps "See average prices and the week's best finds" (line 593), buys in the paywall sheet, which self-dismisses on completion (PaywallView.swift:242-243). `isPro` is now true, so the unlock CTA at lines 592-607 disappears and the `isPro` branch at line 569 takes over — but `trends` is still the cached free payload, so `trends.notableFinds.isEmpty` is true (no "Notable finds" section, line 570) and every `row.averageEstimate` is nil (no "avg $…" on any category row, line 626). The user pays and the card loses content instead of gaining it, for up to 30 minutes or until the app is relaunched.

**Fix.** Key the client cache on tier and re-fetch on entitlement change. In `TrendsAPIClient`, store `cached: (value: Trends, at: Date, isPro: Bool)?` and take the tier as a `fetch(isPro:)` parameter, treating a tier mismatch as a miss (or add `func invalidate()` and call it from the existing `SnapWorthApp.swift:85` `onChange(of: purchaseService.isSubscribed)` handler). Then make the view re-request: `.task(id: purchaseService.isSubscribed) { trends = try? await TrendsAPIClient.shared.fetch(isPro: purchaseService.isSubscribed) }` at HistoryView.swift:239.

### **[FIXED]** The captions that say what the headline money figure means are 2.09:1 — dimmed sage on a sage tint

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/HistoryView.swift:350`

`Color.snapSage.opacity(...)` is used as a text colour on a `Color.snapSage.opacity(0.10)` card. Measured on the composited ground: HistoryView.swift:350 ("N items scanned", 0.7) = 2.09:1; HistoryView.swift:342 ("Your finds are worth", 0.8) = 2.36:1; FlipsView.swift:98 ("N items sold", 0.7) = 2.09:1; FlipsView.swift:88 ("Profit this month" / "All-time profit", 0.85) = 2.52:1. All four are `snapCaption` (13pt) and all four are the label that tells the user what the large number above or below it is. In dark mode the 0.7 sites are still 3.99:1. The full-strength sage headline itself (HistoryView.swift:345, FlipsView.swift:91) is 3.06:1 and squeaks past only because 36-38pt qualifies as large text. `snapWarmGray` on the same tint measures 5.13:1 light / 6.44:1 dark, so a compliant colour was already available.

**What happens.** Open My Finds in light mode: the portfolio banner reads "Your finds are worth / $1,240 / 12 items scanned" with the first and third lines at 2.36:1 and 2.09:1 — under half of AA's floor, and the worst contrast anywhere in the app. Same on My Flips above the profit figure.

**Fix.** Drop the `.opacity()` on these four and use `Color.snapWarmGray` (5.13:1 on the 10% sage tint) or `Color.snapEspresso` (13.31:1). The tint is only 10% of sage, so warm gray reads as intended against it — dimming the same hue as the background is what collapses the ratio.

### **[FIXED]** `snapWarmGray` was deliberately darkened to 5.7:1 to clear AA, then nine call sites re-dilute it with `.opacity()` back to 2.3–3.9:1 — including Apple's required auto-renew disclosure

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/PaywallView.swift:175`

DesignSystem.swift:59-60 carries the comment "`snapWarmGray` was 3.1:1 on cream (below WCAG AA); darkened to 5.7:1". Measured composites on the light card/ground: PaywallView.swift:175 (0.65, the mandatory auto-renew disclosure at `dmSans(10)`) = 2.75:1; FeedbackView.swift:118 (0.55, character counter) = 2.36:1; FeedbackView.swift:125 (0.6, the "N more characters needed" message that explains why Send is disabled) = 2.51:1; ResultView.swift:1216 (0.5, footer wordmark) = 2.11:1; ResultView.swift:347 and :1010 (0.7) = 3.14:1; ThriftFlipView.swift:392 (0.7, the honest-estimate disclaimer) = 3.03:1; ThriftFlipView.swift:226 and :309 (0.8, the OCR note and the "fees unknown" caveat) = 3.69:1; HistoryView.swift:611 (0.8) = 3.86:1. Dark mode is better but 0.55/0.6/0.65 still land at 3.01–3.83:1. Every one of these is a caveat, a legal term or a validation message — the copy where being unreadable matters most.

**What happens.** Read the auto-renew terms on the paywall in light mode: 10pt text at 2.75:1. Or type five characters into Send Feedback — the message telling you five more are needed renders at 2.51:1 while the Send button sits dimmed with no other explanation.

**Fix.** Remove `.opacity()` from every foreground use of `snapWarmGray` and let the token carry its measured 5.68:1. Where a genuinely quieter tone is wanted, add a `snapWarmGrayQuiet` token with a measured value per theme rather than an alpha, and add a test asserting each composite clears 4.5:1 — the widget target already has exactly that test shape (`test_dimmedCreamStillClearsAAOnTheTile`, SnapWorthTests.swift:1677).

### **[FIXED]** "Why this price" price ladder is never condition-adjusted, so it contradicts the headline range after any condition correction

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/ResultView.swift:1249`

The headline range (line 708, `ValueRangeView(low: result.displayValueLow, high: result.displayValueHigh)`) is condition-scaled: `displayValueLow/High` go through `ScanResult.priceRange(for:)`, which multiplies by `condition.priceMultiplier / baselineCondition.priceMultiplier`. The four-point ladder rendered directly above the condition chips is not. `ValuationDetailView` (line 1228) takes only `detail` and prints `row.value` raw (line 1249) from `ValuationDetail.ladder` (ios/SnapWorth/Services/ScanAPIClient.swift:236-243), which is the server's `worst_case/quick_sale/expected/best_case` for the grade the model read — with no factor applied and no label saying so. Nothing in the panel is re-derived when the chip changes; `conditionChip` (line 282) updates the model, the portfolio value, the widget and the share card, but the ladder just re-renders the same server numbers. `factsRow` (line 1351) has the same problem in text form: it keeps printing the AI's `conditionGrade` after the user has overridden it.

**What happens.** Pro user, AI graded the item `good` with est_value 40–90 and expected 65 / best case 100. They tap "Used" to correct it: the headline becomes $31–$70 while the ladder immediately below still reads Floor/Quick sale/Expected $65/Best case $100 — a "best case" 43% above the headline's high end. Tapping "New" inverts it: headline $54–$122 with a ladder "Best case" of $100, below the range's own top. The panel whose job is to explain the price is the one surface on screen showing a different price.

**Fix.** Scale the ladder with the same factor the range uses. Expose it on the model (e.g. `var conditionFactor: Decimal { condition.priceMultiplier / baselineCondition.priceMultiplier }`, the exact quotient `priceRange(for:)` already computes at ScanResult.swift:198), pass it into `ValuationDetailView(detail:factor:)`, and render `Self.money(row.value * factor)`. Same for the `conditionGrade` entry in `factsRow` — drop it from `facts` when `result.condition != result.baselineCondition`, or label it "AI read".

### **[FIXED]** A tag re-read replaces the estimate but never re-renders the cached share card, so "Result card" shares the pre-re-read number

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/ResultView.swift:838`

`ResultViewModel.shareCard` (ResultViewModel.swift:39-42) is an eagerly rendered `UIImage` of `ShareCardView`, produced once in `.task(id: result.id)` (ResultView.swift:203-209) and refreshed only from two places: `onChange(of: paidPriceText)` (ResultView.swift:218) and the condition chip (ResultView.swift:293). `rescan(withTag:)` calls `result.applySharpened(response)` at ResultView.swift:838, which overwrites `itemName`, `valueLow`, `valueHigh`, `conditionNotes` and `valuationDetailData` (ScanResult.swift:83-94) — and therefore `baselineCondition`, `displayValueLow`, `formattedRange` and `findBadge`. Nothing in that success path calls `vm.prepareShareCard`/`vm.scheduleShareCardUpdate`, `.task(id: result.id)` does not re-fire because `result.id` is unchanged, and there is no `onChange` on any value property (only paid/sold/fees text at ResultView.swift:215-226). The "Guess-the-price story" branch is safe because `renderGuessCards` (ResultViewModel.swift:47-55) renders on demand; only the eagerly cached "Result card" branch (ResultView.swift:170-173) is stale. The in-app UI updates correctly and even announces "Re-read with the tag. Estimate updated." (line 845-847), which is what makes the divergence invisible to the user.

**What happens.** Scan a jacket → app shows "$45–$90". Tap "Add tag photo", re-read; the backend returns 300/400, the app now shows "$300–$400" and the new item name, and posts the "Estimate updated" announcement. Tap Share → "Result card". The PNG handed to `ActivityShareSheet` is `vm.shareCard`, still the bitmap rendered before the re-read: old item name, "$45–$90", and a badge computed from the old `displayValueLow`. The user posts a number the app no longer states anywhere.

**Fix.** Add `vm.scheduleShareCardUpdate(result: result, photo: photo)` immediately after `result.applySharpened(response)` in the success branch of `rescan(withTag:)` (ResultView.swift:838). Belt-and-braces: drop the eager cache and render the result card on demand in the confirmation-dialog action the way `renderGuessCards` already does, so no code path can share a bitmap older than the model it depicts.

### **[FIXED]** Cream on a terracotta fill is 3.18:1 in light mode and 2.49:1 in dark — every filled CTA and selected chip, and an in-repo test already asserts the dark pair fails

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/ResultView.swift:304`

`Color.snapOnAccent` is a fixed cream (`SnapDarkHex.cream` = FBF7F2) and it is drawn on `Color.snapTerracotta` (light D96C47, dark E8845F). Measured: 3.18:1 light, 2.49:1 dark. WCAG AA needs 4.5:1 at these sizes (13-17 iOS pt semibold is well under the 18.66pt-bold large-text threshold). Sites in scope: ResultView.swift:304/307 (condition chips), :396/399 (flip-status chips), :1022/1025 (PRO badge), :1043/1046 (marketplace chips); ScanView.swift:70/73 ("Upgrade to Pro"); ThriftFlipView.swift:171/174; HistoryView.swift:430/433 ("Start scanning"); FlipsView.swift:349/351, :381/386; SettingsView.swift:251/258 ("Upgrade"). The shared `PrimaryButton` (DesignSystem.swift:327/332) is the same pairing, so the paywall CTA, "Reveal the estimate", "Add the tag" and every unlock button are included. This is not a judgement call: SnapWorthTests.swift:1705-1708 `test_theForegroundTerracottaWouldFailAsAFill()` asserts `contrast(SnapDarkHex.cream, SnapDarkHex.terracotta) < 3.0` with the comment "why the split exists" — and `Color.snapTerracotta` in dark mode *is* `SnapDarkHex.terracotta`. The split was made for the widget target and on the website (AUDIT-2026-09-07 W-8, AUDIT-2026-09-11 E-10) and never for the app's own UI.

**What happens.** Switch the phone to dark mode and open any result sheet: the selected condition chip's "Like New" label sits on the terracotta fill at 2.49:1, and so does the paywall's "Start Free Trial". In light mode the same labels are 3.18:1. The repo's own unit test declares this exact colour pair unusable as a fill under cream.

**Fix.** Add a `snapTerracottaFill` token and use it wherever `snapOnAccent` sits on terracotta. `SnapDarkHex.terracottaFill` = A8482C already exists for this and measures 5.43:1 under cream in both themes; reuse it rather than inventing a value. Note `lightHighContrast: BE5433` (DesignSystem.swift:49) is not sufficient — cream on it is 4.37:1, and it only applies under Increased Contrast anyway. Extend `test_creamClearsAAOnEveryFilledAccent` to cover the app's fill token so this cannot regress.

### **[FIXED]** Terracotta as a text colour is 3.39:1 on a card and 3.18:1 on the app ground — about a dozen interactive labels and every error message

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/ResultView.swift:199`

`Color.snapTerracotta` (light D96C47) is used directly as a foreground on white cards (3.39:1) and on `snapBackground` cream (3.18:1), below AA's 4.5:1 for all of these sizes. Sites: ResultView.swift:199 (the keyboard toolbar's "Done" — the only way to dismiss the decimal pad on the money screen), :789 (`tagError`), :1132 ("Regenerate"); HistoryView.swift:193 ("Edit"/"Done"), :357 (the insight line), :602 ("See average prices and the week's best finds"); ThriftFlipView.swift:45 ("Close"), :50 ("New item"), :57 (keyboard "Done"), :100 ("Choose from library"), :108 (`scanError`), :186/:217 ("Scan tag"); FeedbackView.swift:214 ("Copy message and address"). This is the same colour pair the website was corrected for in AUDIT-2026-09-07 W-8 (fixed with `--terra-text: #A94E29` at 6.1:1) and AUDIT-2026-09-11 E-10 (`--terra-fill` split from `--terra-text` because "a fill and a foreground are opposite requirements"). The app received neither half of that split.

**What happens.** Type an amount into "What did you pay?" — the keyboard's "Done" button, the only escape from the decimal pad, is terracotta on the keyboard toolbar's near-white ground at ~3.3:1. Fail a tag re-read and the explanation of what went wrong renders at 3.39:1 on white.

**Fix.** Add a `snapTerracottaText` token — the light value the website already settled on (#A94E29) measures 6.1:1 — and use it for every terracotta *label*, keeping `snapTerracotta` for fills and strokes (where 3:1 non-text contrast is the applicable bar and it passes). Dark mode's E8845F is 6.38:1 on card and needs no change.

### **[FIXED]** Tapping "Reveal the estimate" announces nothing — the number the whole flow exists for is never spoken

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/ResultView.swift:750`

`revealPrice()` flips `priceRevealed`, which swaps `coveredValueCard` for `revealedValueCard` (line 646-652). The button the user just activated is inside the card that disappears, so VoiceOver focus is destroyed and nothing is announced; `.accessibilityAddTraits(.isSummaryElement)` and `.accessibilitySortPriority(100)` at lines 746-747 affect ordering and screen summaries, not announcements. `GuessFirst.defaultOn = true` (ShareCardView.swift:349), so this is the default path on every fresh scan — a VoiceOver user meets it on their first result. Every other state change in this same file posts an announcement: the condition chip at :296-300, the status chip at :391-392, and the tag re-read at :846-847, the last one added specifically because "a haptic is the whole of the feedback... and said nothing at all to VoiceOver".

**What happens.** With VoiceOver on, scan an item, swipe to "Reveal the estimate" and double-tap. The haptic fires, the card changes, and the user hears nothing at all; they must hunt back up the sheet to find the estimate they asked for.

**Fix.** Post an announcement inside `revealPrice()`, mirroring :846 — `UIAccessibility.post(notification: .announcement, argument: "Estimated resale value \(result.formattedRange). \(result.confidence) confidence." + (quickVerdict.map { " \($0)" } ?? ""))`. Better still, also add an `@AccessibilityFocusState` on the revealed card and move focus to it so the user lands on the value.

### **[FIXED]** Analytics opt-out never reaches the TelemetryDeck SDK — the toggle bypasses the only code that calls setEnabled

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/SettingsView.swift:141`

The toggle is bound straight to `@AppStorage(Analytics.enabledKey)` (line 13), so flipping it writes the UserDefaults key and nothing else. The only code path that propagates the choice into the SDK is the `Analytics.shared.isEnabled` *setter* (Analytics.swift:286-289 → `backend?.setEnabled(newValue)` → TelemetryDeckAnalytics.swift:33-35 → `config.analyticsDisabled`), and `Analytics.shared.isEnabled` is never assigned anywhere in the app (grep for `Analytics.shared.isEnabled` in ios/: one hit, the read in AnalyticsBootstrap.swift line 45). `config.analyticsDisabled` is therefore fixed for the whole session at the value it had when `AnalyticsBootstrap.start()` ran. Note the haptics toggle two sections above (line 112) does have the `.onChange` this one is missing. `Config.telemetryDeckAppID` is populated (Config.swift:100), so the backend is live in shipping builds. docs/AUDIT-2026-09-07.md:124 asked for both halves — 'build the config explicitly with analyticsDisabled = !Analytics.shared.isEnabled *and update it live on toggle*'; only the launch-time half landed.

**What happens.** Two concrete failures. (1) Opt-out: user launches the app with analytics on, goes to Settings and turns 'Share anonymous analytics' off. `Analytics.track` stops (it re-reads the key), but `config.analyticsDisabled` stays false, so the SDK keeps emitting its own `TelemetryDeck.Session.started` and `Acquisition.newInstallDetected` signals — each carrying the salted per-device identifier — on every foreground for the rest of the session. That is exactly the leak TelemetryDeckAnalytics.swift:18-23 says `analyticsDisabled` exists to stop, and LegalView.swift:45 promises 'You can turn analytics off at any time in Settings.' (2) Opt-in: a user who launched with the toggle off turns it back on. `analyticsDisabled` is still true from init, so every signal — including our own funnel events, which now pass the facade's guard — is dropped inside the SDK until the next cold launch, silently.

**Fix.** Route the toggle through the facade so the setter runs. Add, mirroring line 112: `.onChange(of: analyticsEnabled) { _, on in Analytics.shared.isEnabled = on }` on the Toggle at line 141 (the setter rewrites the same UserDefaults value and then calls `backend?.setEnabled(on)`), or drop `@AppStorage` here for a `Binding(get:{Analytics.shared.isEnabled}, set:{Analytics.shared.isEnabled = $0})`.

### **[FIXED]** `findBadge` rounds the multiple to nearest, so the badge overstates the find by up to 0.5x against the card's own headline

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/ShareCardView.swift:189`

`let multiple = Int(round(low / paid))` at ShareCardView.swift:189 uses round-to-nearest, then `multiple > 1` gates the badge. The threshold for an "Nx find" claim is therefore `low/paid >= N - 0.5`, not `>= N`. The `guard paid < low` on line 188 only establishes that the ratio exceeds 1.0, so the very first badge the user can earn is already wrong: a 1.5x find renders as "2x find". The badge sits 6pt under the headline range (line 151) and 6pt under the "Paid $X" line (line 146), so the card prints the two numbers that disprove its own claim. This is the same class of defect as the divisor bug the code comment at lines 180-184 and `ProductionHardeningTests.swift:2443` already fixed — the divisor was corrected to `displayValueLow`, the rounding was not. Confirmed not present in docs/AUDIT-v2.md, AUDIT-2026-09.md, AUDIT-2026-09-07.md or AUDIT-2026-09-11.md.

**What happens.** Scan an item the model prices at $45–$90 with a `.good` baseline (no re-grade), then enter a paid price of $30. `displayValueLow` is 45.0, `round(45/30) = round(1.5) = 2`, so the shared PNG reads "Paid $30 →", "$45–$90", and a "2x find" capsule. 45/30 is 1.5x. Same at paid $18 on a $45 low: `round(2.5) = 3` → "3x find" for a 2.5x flip. Every badge on every shared card can be a third higher than the card's own arithmetic supports.

**Fix.** Truncate instead of rounding: `let multiple = Int(low / paid)` (integer-truncating the ratio), keeping the `multiple > 1` gate. The existing test case (`paid: 25`, adjusted low 78 → 3.12) still yields "3x find", so `test_shareBadgeUsesTheSameNumberAsTheHeadlineAboveIt` stays green. Add a case asserting `findBadge(paid: 30)` is nil on a 45/90 `.good` item.

### **[FIXED]** HaulWidget and QuickScanWidget paint their background as content, so tinted Home Screen rendering flattens the widget into one unreadable slab

`ios/SnapWorthWidgets/HaulWidget.swift:56`

`HaulWidgetSmallView` puts a full-bleed `Color.wCharcoal` at the top of its ZStack (HaulWidget.swift:56), `HaulWidgetMediumView` does the same (HaulWidget.swift:100), and `QuickScanWidgetView` puts a full-bleed `LinearGradient` there (QuickScanWidget.swift:38-42). All three ALSO already declare the same background the correct way — `.containerBackground(Color.wCharcoal, for: .widget)` at HaulWidget.swift:207 and `.containerBackground(Color.wTerracotta, for: .widget)` at QuickScanWidget.swift:100 — so the in-content fills are pure duplication. The duplication is not harmless: `containerBackground` is the declaration WidgetKit strips and replaces when the widget is not rendering in `.fullColor`, whereas an opaque shape drawn inside the view body is content. In `.accented` rendering WidgetKit keeps only the alpha coverage of each group and paints the whole default group one flat colour; a full-bleed opaque fill covers 100% of the widget at alpha 1, and the labels sit in that same group, so value, caption and icon are painted the identical colour as the slab behind them. The other four widgets in the bundle (`RecentFindsWidget` MoreWidgets.swift:117, `ScansLeftEntryView` MoreWidgets.swift:213/216, `MonthProfitWidget` MoreWidgets.swift:300, `LockScreenHaulWidget` LockScreenWidgets.swift:146) declare background only through `containerBackground` and are unaffected — the inconsistency is confined to the two oldest widget files.

**What happens.** User turns on Settings > Home Screen & App Library > Customize > Tinted (iOS 18+), which puts Home Screen widgets in `.accented` rendering. The Haul Value widget (small and medium) and Quick Scan render as a featureless tinted rectangle: '$348–$620', '8 items scanned', the 'Last scanned' column and the Scan chip are all drawn in the same flat colour as the full-bleed fill on top of which they sit, so the haul total is invisible. The four newer widgets on the same Home Screen render correctly, so the user sees it as these two widgets being broken.

**Fix.** Delete the in-content fills — `Color.wCharcoal` at HaulWidget.swift:56 and :100, and the `LinearGradient` at QuickScanWidget.swift:38-42 — and let the already-present `containerBackground` modifiers supply the ground. For QuickScan, move the gradient into the modifier: `.containerBackground(for: .widget) { LinearGradient(colors: [.wTerracotta, Color(hex: "B84E2A")], startPoint: .topLeading, endPoint: .bottomTrailing) }`. Then mark the one element that should carry the tint with `.widgetAccentable()` (the header wordmark, as LockScreenWidgets.swift:110 already does) so accented mode has two groups rather than one.

### **[FIXED]** The medium Haul widget's 'Scan' capsule is a fake button that opens History, and VoiceOver focuses it as its own element

`ios/SnapWorthWidgets/HaulWidget.swift:166`

`Label("Scan", systemImage: "camera.fill")` at line 166 is styled as an affordance — terracotta capsule, `.clipShape(Capsule())`, white text (lines 169-172). It is not a control. `HaulWidget` declares exactly one destination, `.widgetURL(URL(string: "snapworth://history"))` at line 206, and `Link` is never used, so every tap anywhere in the widget — including on the chip — deep-links to History. Because `HaulWidgetMediumView` has no `.accessibilityElement` grouping anywhere in its body, 'Scan' is also a separately focusable VoiceOver element, so it reads as the widget's primary action. The widget is `.systemMedium` (line 211), which does support multiple `Link` tap targets, so the chip could have been wired up. Compare `QuickScanWidget`, which is honest: its whole surface says 'Scan now' and its `widgetURL` is `snapworth://scan` (QuickScanWidget.swift:99).

**What happens.** User (VoiceOver or not) with at least one scan in their library taps the terracotta 'Scan' pill on the medium Haul Value widget expecting the camera. The app opens on the History tab instead. Under VoiceOver the mismatch is sharper: the element announces 'Scan' with no other context, double-tap lands on History, and there is no way to discover that the whole widget is one History link.

**Fix.** Either make it real — wrap the chip in `Link(destination: URL(string: "snapworth://scan")!) { ... }`, which `.systemMedium` supports — or, if the widget is meant to be History-only, delete the capsule styling and add `.accessibilityHidden(true)` so it stops reading as an action. Do not leave a button-shaped element whose tap goes somewhere else.

### **[FIXED]** "Scans left" renders an unknown allowance as zero — a never-launched app shows "0 free scans left today"

`ios/SnapWorthWidgets/MoreWidgets.swift:164`

Every read of the count coerces nil to 0: `headline` (line 164), `subtitle` (line 171), `circularValue` (line 178), `accentForRemaining` (line 182) and `spokenLabel` (line 189). But nil does not mean zero — the model's own doc comment (SnapWorthWidgets.swift:49) says "`nil` means Pro, or never established", and `WidgetHaulData.empty` carries `freeScansRemaining: nil` (SnapWorthWidgets.swift:66). `WidgetReader.readHaul()` returns `.empty` whenever the App Group holds no blob or the decode fails (SnapWorthWidgets.swift:259-263), and nothing in the entry distinguishes "never written" from "written with zero left" — `updatedAt` is stored but no widget reads it. The zero branch is also the alarming one: line 182 paints the number terracotta and line 172 says "Back tomorrow, or go Pro".

**What happens.** A user installs SnapWorth and adds the "Scans left" widget from the gallery before ever opening the app (or before `seedWidgetData()` has run). No blob exists, `readHaul()` returns `.empty`, and the widget shows a large terracotta "0" captioned "Back tomorrow, or go Pro" — and the circular complication shows "0" with VoiceOver saying "No free scans left today" — to someone whose full daily allowance is untouched. Same output after any decode failure of a truncated blob.

**Fix.** Treat nil as unknown rather than zero. Add an explicit branch before the `?? 0` reads: when `haul.freeScansRemaining == nil && !haul.isPro`, render "—" with "Open SnapWorth" (or the compiled `freeScansAllowed`), keep the neutral `wWarmGray` accent instead of `wTerracotta`, and speak "Scan count not available yet". Fold the same nil branch into `circularValue` and `spokenLabel`.

### **[FIXED]** The daily allowance resets but no timeline entry is scheduled at the reset, so "Scans left" shows a stale 0 for up to a day

`ios/SnapWorthWidgets/MoreWidgets.swift:27`

`HaulOnlyProvider.getTimeline` (lines 26-30) emits exactly one entry dated `.now` and a blind `policy: .after(now + 1h)`. `freeScansRemaining` is a snapshot the *app* took at write time (WidgetDataStore.swift:326), so each hourly refresh re-reads and re-renders the same number. The extension cannot recompute it even in principle: `FreeScanCounter` stores its day stamp and counts in `UserDefaults.standard` (ScanViewModel.swift:298-341), not in the App Group suite the widget can reach, and it resets on the *UTC* day (`isServerToday`, ScanViewModel.swift:291-295). Nothing in the blob records when the allowance next resets, and nothing schedules an entry there. The same hourly-only policy is repeated in LockScreenWidgets.swift:33, HaulWidget.swift:23 and QuickScanWidget.swift:25.

**What happens.** A free user in UTC-7 spends their one free scan at 09:00 Monday local. The blob records `freeScansRemaining: 0`. The server allowance resets at 17:00 Monday local (UTC midnight). They don't reopen the app. The "Scans left" widget keeps reading "0 — Back tomorrow, or go Pro" through Monday evening and all of Tuesday; the hourly refreshes change nothing because they re-read the same cached snapshot. It only corrects when the app is next launched and `seedWidgetData()` runs.

**Fix.** Store the reset instant in the blob — the server already exposes it as `X-Quota-Resets-At` (see AUDIT-2026-09-07 I-4), or compute the UTC midnight following `updatedAt` — and have `HaulOnlyProvider` emit two entries: the current snapshot, plus one dated at that reset carrying the full allowance, with `policy: .after(resetDate)` when the reset is sooner than an hour out. As a belt-and-braces measure, have the view fall back to the nil/unknown branch of the previous finding once `now` falls in a later UTC day than `updatedAt`, so the widget degrades to "—" rather than asserting a number it cannot know.

### **[FIXED]** Quick Scan's 10pt caption is 2.93:1 on the terracotta gradient — below even the 3:1 non-text floor

`ios/SnapWorthWidgets/QuickScanWidget.swift:77`

`Color.wBackground.opacity(0.65)` over the gradient's lower stop `#B84E2A` composites to `rgb(227,189,175)`, which against `#B84E2A` measures 2.93:1 — used for the 10pt caption at lines 76-77 ('8 items in your haul') and 80-81 ('Find out what it's worth'). The 11pt wordmark at lines 48-49 uses `opacity(0.75)` over the gradient's upper stop `#C9583A` and measures 2.95:1. WCAG AA requires 4.5:1 at these sizes; both are worse than the 3.39:1 white-on-terracotta failure the prior audit already raised for the website (docs/AUDIT-2026-09-07.md, W-8), and that one at least met the 3:1 graphics floor. Full-opacity `Color.wBackground` does not rescue it either: `#FAF9F7` on `#C9583A` is only 4.04:1, so the gradient itself is too light for AA text.

**What happens.** Any user with the Quick Scan widget on their Home Screen: the only line telling them what the widget is tracking ('8 items in your haul') and the wordmark identifying which app it belongs to are both under 3:1 at 10-11pt. In sunlight, at reduced display brightness, or for a user with low vision or reduced contrast sensitivity, both lines disappear against the gradient and the widget reads as an unlabeled orange square.

**Fix.** Two changes: drop the alpha (use full `Color.wBackground` at lines 49, 77 and 81), and darken the gradient so white clears 4.5:1. `#A94E29` — the AA-compliant terracotta the website already defines as `--terra-text` per docs/AUDIT-2026-09-07.md, W-8 — gives 5.24:1 against `#FAF9F7`. Using it for both gradient stops fixes the caption and the wordmark together, and reuses an existing token rather than inventing a colour.

### **[FIXED]** Every Dynamic Island presentation of the thrift run is unlabeled; the minimal one announces only an SF Symbol name

`ios/SnapWorthWidgets/SnapWorthWidgetsLiveActivity.swift:29`

All four Dynamic Island presentations ship raw content with no `.accessibilityLabel`: expanded leading is `Label("\(context.state.itemCount)", systemImage: "camera.viewfinder")` (line 29), expanded trailing is a bare `Text(context.state.formattedRange)` (line 34), `compactTrailing` is a bare `Text("\(context.state.itemCount)")` (line 58), and `minimal` is an `Image(systemName: "camera.viewfinder")` with nothing else (line 62). The Lock Screen presentation of the same activity got a hand-authored label (line 120), and `LockScreenCircularView` carries a comment explaining exactly why a bare icon-plus-number is unacceptable (LockScreenWidgets.swift:81-86) — so the pattern was understood and then not applied to the Island. This is the Live Activity written in the last 24 hours with no human review, and the data-layer unit tests cannot see a view's accessibility tree.

**What happens.** A thrift run is active, VoiceOver is on, user swipes to the Dynamic Island. Collapsed, it announces 'camera viewfinder' and '6' as two unrelated fragments. Minimal presentation (another Live Activity is also running) announces only 'camera viewfinder' — no app name, no count, no value, nothing identifying which activity is focused. Expanded, the trailing region announces '$120–$240' as a naked amount with no indication it is the run total. The same information is fully available to a sighted user.

**Fix.** Add labels per region. Line 29: `.accessibilityLabel("\(context.state.findsLabel) this run")`. Line 34: `.accessibilityLabel("Worth \(spokenRange)")`. `compactTrailing` (line 58) and `minimal` (line 62): wrap each in `.accessibilityElement(children: .ignore)` + `.accessibilityLabel("Thrift run, \(context.state.findsLabel)")`. Mark the decorative `camera.viewfinder` in `compactLeading` (line 53) `.accessibilityHidden(true)` so it is not announced separately from the count beside it.

### **[FIXED]** `staleDate` is set on every request and update but the widget never reads `context.isStale`

`ios/SnapWorthWidgets/SnapWorthWidgetsLiveActivity.swift:16`

`ThriftRunController` sets `staleDate: now + 90 minutes` on both `Activity.request` (line 47) and every `activity.update` (line 95). `staleDate` does not change how the system renders a Live Activity — it only flips `context.isStale` to true and leaves the presentation entirely to the widget. This configuration uses `context.state` and `context.attributes` (lines 16-17, 29, 34, 44, 58) and never reads `context.isStale`, in either the Lock Screen branch or the `dynamicIsland` branch (line 26). The controller's comment on lines 20-22 ("the system dims the Activity, signalling 'this may be out of date'") describes behaviour ActivityKit does not provide, so nothing anywhere implements the signal.

**What happens.** User starts a run at 9:00, scans two items worth $120–$260 by 9:10, then browses for two hours without scanning. At 10:40 the content is stale. The Lock Screen still renders "$120–$260" in full-strength `Color.wSage` with "2 finds this trip" (lines 87-98) and `Text(startedAt, style: .timer)` (line 106) still ticking up, pixel-identical to a live run. Because `pushType` is nil (ThriftRunController.swift:48) and the app is not running, nothing can correct it either — the user reads a 90-minute-old money figure as current while deciding whether to buy the next thing.

**Fix.** Pass `context.isStale` into `ThriftRunLockScreenView` and branch on it: dim the range and replace "N finds this trip" with an as-of qualifier, and do the same in `DynamicIslandExpandedRegion(.trailing)`/`(.bottom)`. Drop or correct the claim in ThriftRunController.swift:20-22.

## Medium

### `isConfigured` is set ~300ms late on the MainActor, so a second `requestPermissionAndSetup()` reconfigures the same session and fires a bogus "Camera setup failed" alert

`ios/SnapWorth/Camera/CameraManager.swift:41`

`setupSessionIfNeeded()` reads `isConfigured` on the MainActor at line 41, but `setupSession()` only sets it at line 93 — inside a `Task { @MainActor }` dispatched after the entire session-queue block has run, including the blocking `session.startRunning()` at line 92 (200-500ms on real hardware, plus device discovery and input creation). Any second call to `requestPermissionAndSetup()` inside that window sees `isConfigured == false` and enqueues a second full configuration pass, which builds a second `AVCaptureDeviceInput` for the same camera. A plain `AVCaptureSession` cannot hold two video inputs (that needs `AVCaptureMultiCamSession`), so `canAddInput(input)` at line 58 returns false, the guard fails, and `error = .setupFailed` is published — while the preview from the first pass is running perfectly. It also triggers the unbalanced-`beginConfiguration` bug above on a session that is otherwise healthy.

**What happens.** ScanView is a TabView tab (MainTabView.swift:39) and its `onAppear` (ScanView.swift:247) calls `requestPermissionAndSetup()` on every appearance. Open the Scan tab, switch to My Finds and back within about half a second of the first open — or grant camera permission and immediately switch tabs and back — and the second pass runs: the user gets the modal "Camera setup failed. Please restart the app." on top of a working camera, and the session is left inside an open configuration block.

**Fix.** Claim the flag on the actor that reads it, before the async work: set `isConfigured = true` at the top of `setupSession()` (it is already MainActor-isolated) and set it back to false on the failure path inside the queue block; or make the queue block itself idempotent with `guard self.session.inputs.isEmpty else { return }` after `beginConfiguration()`.

### `capturePhoto` drops the tap silently when the session isn't running, while the shutter haptic still fires

`ios/SnapWorth/Camera/CameraManager.swift:125`

`capturePhoto()` opens with `guard let self, self.session.isRunning else { return }` (line 125): no error published, no delegate callback, nothing observable. But the shutter's only readiness gate is `authStatus` (ScanView.swift:189), which flips to `.authorized` long before the session is actually running — both `setupSession()` and `startSession()` hop onto `sessionQueue`, and `startRunning()` blocks that queue for 200-500ms. Inside that window `session.isRunning` is false, yet `Haptics.capture()` at ScanView.swift:177 has already fired, so the device confirms a photo that was never taken and the UI stays on the viewfinder.

**What happens.** Two ordinary repros. (a) Open the Scan tab and tap the shutter immediately: haptic, no capture, no error, no analysing overlay. (b) The thrifting loop — dismiss the result sheet, which restarts the session asynchronously via the `isCameraObscured` onChange (ScanView.swift:264-269), then tap for the next item straight away: same silent drop. It is the trigger for the double-tap damage above, and combined with the missing interruption handling it becomes permanent rather than transient.

**Fix.** Publish readiness: `@Published private(set) var isSessionRunning` updated on the session queue immediately after `startRunning()`/`stopRunning()` (or KVO on `session.isRunning`), and add `|| !cameraManager.isSessionRunning` to the shutter's `.disabled`. In the guard's failure path set `error = .captureFailed` instead of returning silently, and move `Haptics.capture()` to fire only once the capture has actually been issued.

### No interruption or runtime-error observers: once the session stops for a reason other than our own code, the camera is dead until the app is force-quit

`ios/SnapWorth/Camera/CameraManager.swift:97`

`startSession()`/`stopSession()` (lines 97-109) are the only session controls in the file, and `CameraManager` registers no notification observers at all — `AVCaptureSessionWasInterrupted`, `AVCaptureSessionInterruptionEnded` and `AVCaptureSessionRuntimeError` are never observed. ScanView has no `scenePhase` hook either; its only lifecycle entry points are `onAppear` (line 246), `onDisappear` (line 258) and the `isCameraObscured` onChange (line 264). `.videoDeviceInUseByAnotherClient` does not auto-resume (Apple's own AVCam sample exposes a manual Resume button precisely for it), and a runtime error carrying `AVError.mediaServicesWereReset` requires the app to call `startRunning()` again.

**What happens.** Another client takes the camera while the app is foreground (iPad compatibility mode in Split View, a FaceTime/Siri grab) or mediaserverd resets. `session.isRunning` goes false, the preview freezes on its last frame, and because `capturePhoto` returns silently at line 125 every subsequent shutter tap only vibrates. `onAppear` cannot rescue it — the view never disappeared, so `startSession()` is never called again, and the `isCameraObscured` onChange only fires if the user happens to open and close a sheet. The user's only fix is force-quitting the app.

**Fix.** Subscribe to the three notifications in `init` and republish an interruption state the UI can render ("Camera unavailable — tap to resume"); call `startSession()` on `.interruptionEnded`, and on a runtime error whose `AVError` code is `.mediaServicesWereReset`. Add a `scenePhase == .active` restart in ScanView as a backstop.

### isConfigured is a check-then-act across an actor hop, so the capture session can be configured twice

`ios/SnapWorth/Camera/CameraManager.swift:41`

`setupSessionIfNeeded()` reads the MainActor-isolated `isConfigured` at line 41 and, when false, calls `setupSession()`, which dispatches the whole configuration onto `sessionQueue` (line 49). The flag is only set true at line 93, from a `Task { @MainActor ... }` that hops back *after* `beginConfiguration`/`addInput`/`addOutput`/`commitConfiguration`/`startRunning` have all finished on the queue — on a real device that is several hundred milliseconds. So the window between "setup started" and "isConfigured == true" is wide open, and a second `requestPermissionAndSetup()` in that window passes the guard and enqueues `setupSession()` again. The second queue block calls `self.session.beginConfiguration()` (line 51), builds an `AVCaptureDeviceInput` for the same back wide-angle camera, and then `self.session.canAddInput(input)` returns false because the session already has an input for that device — so the `guard` at lines 55-59 fails, `error = .setupFailed` is published, and the block `return`s at line 61 without ever calling `commitConfiguration()`. The same unbalanced-begin path is reachable with no race at all on any device where `AVCaptureDevice.default(.builtInWideAngleCamera, for: .video, position: .back)` returns nil or the input cannot be created (iPad in compatibility mode, camera held by another app, Screen Time camera restriction).

**What happens.** Cold-launch with camera permission already granted. `ScanView.onAppear` calls `requestPermissionAndSetup()` (ScanView.swift:247) and setup begins. Switch to the "My Finds" tab and back to "Scan" before the session finishes starting — `onDisappear` enqueues `stopSession()` (ScanView.swift:258) and the second `onAppear` calls `requestPermissionAndSetup()` again with `isConfigured` still false. Queue order becomes [setup1][stop][setup2]; setup2 hits `canAddInput == false`, so the user gets the "Camera setup failed. Please restart the app." alert (CameraManager.swift:189, surfaced by ScanView.swift:327-330) on a camera that was working, and the session is left inside an uncommitted `beginConfiguration()` so the later `startSession()` from `onChange(of: isCameraObscured)` (ScanView.swift:268) cannot bring the preview back.

**Fix.** Two changes. (1) Close the check-then-act window: set `isConfigured = true` synchronously in `setupSessionIfNeeded()` before dispatching (and reset it to false in the failure branch), or move the guard onto `sessionQueue` so the flag is read and written on the same serial queue rather than across a MainActor hop. (2) Balance the transaction: every early return inside the `sessionQueue.async` block — the `guard` at :55-59 and the output branch — must call `self.session.commitConfiguration()` before returning, e.g. by wrapping the body in `defer { self.session.commitConfiguration() }` immediately after `beginConfiguration()`.

### The streak named in the free-scan body is frozen at schedule time, so a reminder scheduled for tomorrow asserts a streak that is already broken when it fires

`ios/SnapWorth/Services/NotificationManager.swift:300`

Line 300-301 computes `freeScanBody(streak:)` once, at schedule time, and freezes it into the request. `syncEligible` passes `ScanStreak.current()` (line 349), which by its own definition (ios/SnapWorth/ViewModels/ScanViewModel.swift:213-221) returns the stored count when the last scan was today **or yesterday**. When `nextFreeScanDate` (lines 306-314) returns *tomorrow* because today's hour has already passed, and no scan happened today, the streak that is alive now will be dead by the fire date. The body then claims a day number that cannot be reached — directly against the rule stated in the comment at lines 317-318: "No guilt when it broke: the streak simply isn't mentioned."

**What happens.** User's last scan was yesterday, `snapworth_streak_count` = 4, reminder hour 18:00. They foreground the app today at 20:00 and do not scan. `syncEligible` → `syncFreeScanReminder(scannedToday: false, streak: ScanStreak.current() == 4)`; today's 18:00 has passed so the fire date is tomorrow 18:00, with the frozen body "Day 5 of your streak is waiting — your free scan is back." Tomorrow at 18:00 the last scan is two days old, `ScanStreak.current()` returns 0, and a scan then can only produce day 1. The notification names a streak the app has already discarded.

**Fix.** Pass the streak as of the fire date, not `now`: in `syncFreeScanReminder`, if the resolved `fireDate` is not on the same calendar day as `now` and `ScanStreak.scannedToday()` is false, the streak will be 0 at delivery — use `freeScanBody(streak: 0)`. Only name the streak when the reminder fires on a day the streak is still alive (fires today, or the user scanned today).

### The trial reminder is re-anchored to wall-clock components, so a device time-zone change detaches it from the absolute trial deadline

`ios/SnapWorth/Services/NotificationManager.swift:467`

Lines 467-469 convert every fire date to `[.year, .month, .day, .hour, .minute]` with no `.timeZone`, so `UNCalendarNotificationTrigger` resolves it against whatever zone the device is in at delivery. That is exactly right for recap, ledger, portfolio and freeScan, which all mean "10:00 / 18:00 local". It is wrong for `.trial`, whose fire date is derived from an absolute instant — `Calendar.current.date(byAdding: .hour, value: -24, to: endDate)` at line 253. Stripping the zone turns "24 hours before the subscription converts" into "this wall-clock time, whatever it then means", and the body at line 260 hard-codes "ends tomorrow".

**What happens.** Trial ends 2026-09-20 09:00 UTC. Device is on Asia/Tokyo when `syncTrialReminder` runs (ios/SnapWorth/Services/StoreKitPurchaseService.swift:285): fireDate = 2026-09-19 18:00 JST, stored as components (2026-09-19, 18:00). The user flies to America/Los_Angeles and does not reopen the app, so nothing re-syncs. iOS fires the trigger at 2026-09-19 18:00 PDT = 2026-09-20 01:00 UTC — 8 hours before the charge, not 24, and the notification says the trial "ends tomorrow" when it ends the same day. A 16-hour zone swing moves the courtesy warning into the window where the user can no longer cancel before the first bill.

**Fix.** Special-case the absolute-deadline category: for `.trial` use `UNTimeIntervalNotificationTrigger(timeInterval: fireDate.timeIntervalSinceNow, repeats: false)`, or set `comps.timeZone = Calendar.current.timeZone` (and include `.second`) so the components stay pinned to the instant they were computed from. Leave the wall-clock conversion for the four local-time categories.

### resolveDailyCap reads the pending-request set across suspension points, so the one-per-day cap silently fails

`ios/SnapWorth/Services/NotificationManager.swift:486`

`NotificationManager` is `@MainActor`, which serialises its code only *between* awaits. `add(id:category:fireDate:body:)` suspends three times — `await isAuthorized()` (:458), `await resolveDailyCap(...)` which itself suspends at `let pending = await center.pendingNotificationRequests()` (:486), and `try await center.add(request)` (:473). `resolveDailyCap` then makes its whole decision (lines 489-501: evict lower-priority same-day requests, or return false because an equal/higher one owns the day) from that `pending` snapshot, and acts on it at :503 and :473 — a read-modify-write straddling two suspensions on state that lives outside the app (the notification daemon). Two `add` calls that interleave at those awaits therefore each read a snapshot that does not contain the other's request, each conclude the day is free, and each schedule. The class doc at lines 20-30 and the `resolveDailyCap` doc at :480-483 both state the invariant this breaks: "at most one notification per calendar day across all categories". Every scheduling entry point is an independent unstructured `Task`, so the interleave is not hypothetical — `ScanViewModel.startScan` creates two of them back to back on the MainActor: the free-scan reminder via `noteScanForStreakAndReminder` (ScanViewModel.swift:263) and the monthly recap (ScanViewModel.swift:128). `StoreKitPurchaseService.init`'s `Task` (StoreKitPurchaseService.swift:45) reaching `syncTrialReminder` (:285) likewise races `MainTabView`'s scenePhase `.active` `Task` running `syncEligible` (MainTabView.swift:89), and `Transaction.updates` (StoreKitPurchaseService.swift:288-297) can fire `refreshSubscriptionStatus` → `syncTrialReminder` at any moment.

**What happens.** A free-tier user with the opt-in free-scan reminder enabled completes their third-or-later scan of the month on the last day of the month. `startScan` fires Task A (`syncFreeScanReminder`, target = tomorrow at 18:00 — the 1st) and Task B (`scheduleMonthlyRecap`, target = the 1st at 10:00). Both reach `add`, both suspend at `await isAuthorized()`, both then call `pendingNotificationRequests()` and get a snapshot with neither request present. A resumes, sees nothing on the 1st, adds `freeScan.daily`. B resumes with its stale snapshot, also sees nothing on the 1st, and adds `recap.monthly`. The user gets two SnapWorth notifications on the 1st, and `notification_scheduled` is tracked twice (:474) for a day the cap says can hold one. With the priorities as written (freeScan 2 > recap 1) the recap should have been dropped.

**Fix.** Serialise the scheduling path instead of relying on MainActor mutual exclusion. Simplest: give the class a `private var scheduling: Task<Void, Never>?` and have `add` chain onto it (`let previous = scheduling; scheduling = Task { await previous?.value; ... }`) so no two `add` bodies can be in flight at once; or move the read-decide-write of `resolveDailyCap` plus the `center.add` into a dedicated `actor` whose single method does all three without yielding to other schedulers. Either way the pending-set snapshot and the request that depends on it must not be separated by a suspension another `add` can slip into.

### **[FIXED]** Vision failure resumes the same checked continuation twice, which traps

`ios/SnapWorth/Services/PriceTagOCR.swift:61`

`recognizeText(_:orientation:)` wraps Vision in `withCheckedThrowingContinuation` and resumes it from two independent places: the `VNRecognizeTextRequest` completion handler (`continuation.resume(throwing: error)` at :47 and `continuation.resume(returning: lines)` at :53) and the `catch` around `handler.perform([request])` (`continuation.resume(throwing: error)` at :61). These are not mutually exclusive. `VNImageRequestHandler.perform(_:)` invokes each request's `completionHandler` — with `error` set when that request failed — and *then* throws that error to its caller. So for a request that fails during execution the completion handler resumes the continuation with the error, `perform` throws, and the `catch` resumes it a second time. A checked continuation resumed twice is a hard trap (`SWIFT TASK CONTINUATION MISUSE: ... tried to resume its continuation more than once`), not a recoverable error, so it takes the app down rather than falling through to the manual-entry path this function exists to enable.

**What happens.** Thrift Flip → "Read the price tag". `ThriftFlipViewModel.readPriceTag` (ThriftFlipViewModel.swift:110-121) calls `PriceTagOCR.detectPrice`, whose `catch` is written to show "Couldn't read the tag — enter the price manually." But if the text request itself errors rather than simply finding nothing — Vision rejecting the `CGImage` mid-request, the request being cancelled, or an unsupported request/revision on the running OS — the completion handler fires with that error and `perform` throws the same error, double-resuming the continuation and crashing the process instead of showing that message. (A photo Vision processes successfully but finds no price in is the `noPriceFound` path at :35-37 and is unaffected.)

**Fix.** Resume exactly once. Drop the completion handler entirely and read the results synchronously after `perform` returns: `try handler.perform([request])` inside the continuation's `do`, then `continuation.resume(returning: (request.results as? [VNRecognizedTextObservation] ?? []).compactMap { ... })`, with a single `catch { continuation.resume(throwing: error) }`. If the handler must stay, guard it with a `resumed` flag (or switch to `withCheckedThrowingContinuation` plus a small class holding the continuation and niling it on first resume).

### Cancelling the Restore sign-in prompt renders a raw StoreKit error string in red on the paywall

`ios/SnapWorth/Services/StoreKitPurchaseService.swift:99`

`restorePurchases()` wraps every `AppStore.sync()` failure into `PurchaseError.failed(error.localizedDescription)`. `AppStore.sync()` throws `StoreKitError.userCancelled` when the customer dismisses the App Store authentication prompt it puts up — a user action, not a failure. That becomes `.failed(...)`, `AppError.from` maps it to `.purchaseFailed(msg)` (AppError.swift:143), whose `errorDescription` is the raw message verbatim (AppError.swift:88-89), and PaywallViewModel.swift:88 assigns it to `errorMessage`, which PaywallView.swift:145-148 renders in `.red`.

The purchase path deliberately does not do this: `purchase()` throws `PurchaseError.cancelled`, AppError maps it to `.purchaseCancelled` with a `nil` description (AppError.swift:86-87), and PaywallViewModel.swift:74 suppresses it explicitly (`if appError != .purchaseCancelled`). The restore path has neither half of that treatment.

**What happens.** User taps "Restore purchase", iOS presents the App Store sign-in sheet, the user taps Cancel because they did not expect a password prompt. `AppStore.sync()` throws `StoreKitError.userCancelled`, and the paywall shows a red error line carrying an unedited framework string (a bare `StoreKit.StoreKitError` description, not user-safe copy written for this app) directly above the Subscribe button — telling a user who deliberately backed out that something went wrong, on the payment screen.

**Fix.** In `restorePurchases()`, catch cancellation before the generic wrap: `catch StoreKitError.userCancelled { throw PurchaseError.cancelled }` ahead of the existing `catch`, and apply the same `if appError != .purchaseCancelled` guard in `PaywallViewModel.restore()` (line 87-89) that `purchase()` already uses at line 74. Also stop tracking `.restoreCompleted` (line 103) on a run that restored nothing, so the metric means what its name says.

### Widget gates month-to-date profit behind Pro, but the app gives the same figure to the free tier — the "Profit this month" widget upsells a feature the user already has

`ios/SnapWorth/Services/WidgetDataStore.swift:440`

`writeHaul` withholds the month ledger from non-subscribers: `monthProfit: pro && !monthProfits.isEmpty ? monthProfit : nil` (line 440) and `monthFlips: pro ? monthProfits.count : 0` (line 441). `MonthProfitView` then renders that nil as an upsell for anyone whose blob says `isPro == false`: "Pro" (MoreWidgets.swift:240), "Track profit with Pro" (:251-252) and VoiceOver "Profit tracking is a Pro feature" (:259-260). No such gate exists in the app. `FlipsView` puts free users on the month scope on purpose — `scope = isPro ? .allTime : .month` (FlipsView.swift:21), header "Profit this month" (:86), the figure at :90 — and `FlipsViewModel.summary` computes exactly the same month-scoped realized profit the widget's writer does (FlipsViewModel.swift:89-92 vs WidgetDataStore.swift:415-423). Free users additionally get six months of profit bars (`monthlyCard`, FlipsView.swift:156-177) and the stats grid (:112-128). The real ledger gates are all-time scope, sold rows past `Config.ledgerFreeSoldCap` (FlipsView.swift:57) and CSV export (:425) — matching AUDIT-2026-09-07 I-26, which enumerates the actual Pro gates (valuation detail, add tag, portfolio trend, ledger export) and does not include profit tracking. The widget extension was never read by any prior audit (AUDIT-2026-09-07:432, AUDIT-2026-09-11:269), so this is not a re-report.

**What happens.** A never-paid user marks one item sold this month and enters what they paid. The Flips tab reads "Profit this month · +$47 · 1 item sold". The "Profit this month" widget on the same Home Screen reads "Pro" / "Track profit with Pro" — and its own deep link (`snapworth://flips`, MoreWidgets.swift:273) opens the exact screen showing the number it claims is paid. A user who subscribes off that widget pays for what they already had; the same blob state is produced for a lapsed subscriber, so the widget also tells them profit tracking has been taken away when it has not.

**Fix.** Write both fields regardless of tier — drop `pro &&` at line 440 and the `pro ?` at line 441; the figure is already month-scoped, which is precisely the free tier's scope — and delete the `haul.isPro` upsell branches at MoreWidgets.swift:240, 251-252 and 259-260, keeping the "No flips sold yet this month" copy for the genuinely-empty case. Note this inverts the committed assertion at ProductionHardeningTests.swift:2555-2561 ("a paid figure outlived the subscription"), which encodes the widget author's assumption rather than the product's gate, so that test must change with it. If month profit is instead *meant* to be Pro, then FlipsView's free month summary is the leak and both surfaces plus the paywall benefit list have to move together — but that is a product decision, not the current behaviour.

### UNUserNotificationCenter delegate is assigned from an async .task, after launch has already finished

`ios/SnapWorth/SnapWorthApp.swift:95`

`registerAsDelegate()` (NotificationManager.swift:133, `center.delegate = self`) is invoked from `.task { }` attached to the `WindowGroup` content (SnapWorthApp.swift:95). A SwiftUI `.task` does not run until the view appears — i.e. after the scene has been connected and launch has completed. `UNUserNotificationCenterDelegate` is documented as having to be assigned before the app finishes launching, precisely because the response for the notification that *caused* the launch is delivered at that point; with no delegate installed there is nothing to receive it. Everything in `handleOpen` (NotificationManager.swift:524-544) and the five `Notification.Name` routes `MainTabView` listens for (MainTabView.swift:65-81) hang off that one delegate callback, so the entire notification deep-link feature is dead on the cold-launch path while working perfectly when the app is already resident — which is exactly the shape of bug that survives manual testing.

**What happens.** With the app not running, tap the monthly-recap notification ("Your September Recap is ready 👀"). The app cold-launches; `userNotificationCenter(_:didReceive:)` (NotificationManager.swift:516) is never called because `center.delegate` is still nil at that moment, so no `snapOpenFlips` is posted, `markRecapViewed()` never runs, `notification_opened` is never tracked, and the user lands on the Scan tab instead of My Flips. Tapping the same notification while the app is in the background works, since the delegate is installed by then. The same applies to the ledger, trial, portfolio and freeScan notifications.

**Fix.** Set the delegate before launch completes rather than from a view task. Call `NotificationManager.shared.registerAsDelegate()` from `SnapWorthApp.init()` (alongside `AnalyticsBootstrap.start()` at :21 and `CrashReporter.shared.start()` at :36 — `init` is already MainActor-isolated, since `View`/`App` conformance makes the type MainActor here), or add a `UIApplicationDelegateAdaptor` and assign it in `application(_:didFinishLaunchingWithOptions:)`. Then delete the `.task` at :95.

### generateListing has no staleness guard, so an in-flight response overwrites a newer marketplace selection

`ios/SnapWorth/ViewModels/ResultViewModel.swift:89`

generateListing reads selectedMarketplace at line 87, awaits the network call, then unconditionally assigns generatedListing = listing at line 89 and tracks .listingGenerated with the *current* selectedMarketplace at line 94.
